n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}:=\left\{[n_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid n_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}\ \forall\varepsilon\right\}.

Mimicking nonstandard analysis, the numbers
n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
are
called
hypernatural
numbers. To glimpse the necessity of studying

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
, it suffices to note that
\frac{1}{n}<\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}

is always false for
n\in\mathbb{N}
but it can be satisfied for suitable

n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
. Therefore, if
\lim_{n\to+\infty}a_{n}=0
in the
classical sense, i.e. for
n\in\mathbb{N}
and with respect to the sharp
topology, then necessarily
a_{n}
is infinitesimal for
n\in\mathbb{N}

sufficiently large. This represents a severe limitation for this notion
of limit. It is also clear from the fact that
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
with the sharp
topology is an ultra-pseudometric space, see e.g.
[
97
]
, and
hence a series in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
converges in the sharp topology if and
only if its general term
a_{n}\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
in the sharp topology, see
[
60
]
.

7.1.
Integration over functionally compact sets

In section
5
, we already defined a notion
of integral over intervals using the notion of primitive. This notion
does not help if we want to define the integral
\int_{D}f
of a
GSF
f
over a domain
D\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
which is more general
than an interval. In this case, it is natural to try an
\varepsilon
-wise
definition of the type
\int_{D}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}:=\left[\int_{D_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
where the net
(f_{\varepsilon})
defines the GSF
f
and the net
(D_{\varepsilon})

determines, in some way, the subset
D\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, e.g.
D=[D_{\varepsilon}]

in case of internal sets. In pursuing this idea, it is important to
recall that the internal set (interval)
[0,1]=\left[[0,1]_{\mathbb{R}}\right]

can also be defined by a net of finite sets. Indeed, if
\text{int}(-)

is the integer part function, and we set

\displaystyle N_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=\text{int}\left(\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1/\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=\{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{1/\varepsilon},2\rho_{\varepsilon}^{1/\varepsilon},\ldots,N_{\varepsilon}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{1/\varepsilon}\}

(7.1)

then the Hausdorff distance
d_{\text{H}}([0,1]_{\mathbb{R}},K_{\varepsilon})=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{1/\varepsilon}

and hence
[0,1]=[K_{\varepsilon}]
(see also
[
115
,
42
]
). Consequently,
if
\lambda
is the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}
, we have that the generalized
number
\left[\lambda([0,1]_{\mathbb{R}})\right]=1
, whereas
\left[\lambda(K_{\varepsilon})\right]=0

and, in general,
\left[\int_{[0,1]_{\mathbb{R}}}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}\right]\neq\left[\int_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}\right]=0
.
Therefore, even the definition of integral over an interval cannot
be easily accomplished by proceeding
\varepsilon
-wise, i.e. on defining
nets.

If we try to understand when such an
\varepsilon
-wise definition can be
accomplished, it turns out that we have to consider an enlargement

\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})
and then take
m\to+\infty
.
This is indeed quite natural if one keeps in mind that
[K_{\varepsilon}]=[L_{\varepsilon}]

if and only if the Hausdorff distance
d_{\text{H}}(K_{\varepsilon},L_{\varepsilon})

defines a negligible nets, (see
[
115
,
42
]
). In the following,
we say that
(K_{\varepsilon})
is a
representative of
K\Subset_{\text{f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

if
K=[K_{\varepsilon}]
,
(K_{\varepsilon})
is sharply bounded, and
K_{\varepsilon}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}

for all
\varepsilon
.

Definition 56
.

Let
\mu
be a Borel measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}

and let
K
be a functionally compact subset of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Then we call
K

\mu
-measurable if the limit

\mu(K):=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\to\infty\\
m\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}))]

(7.2)

exists for some representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
of
K
. The limit is
taken in the sharp topology on
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, and
\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{r}(A):=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:d(x,A)\leq r\}
.

In the following result, we will prove that this definition satisfies
our requirements. We will occasionally integrate generalized functions
more general than GSF:

Definition 57
.

Let
K\Subset_{\text{f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Let
(\Omega_{\varepsilon})

be a net of open subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
(f_{\varepsilon})
be a net
of continuous maps
f_{\varepsilon}
:
\Omega_{\varepsilon}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
.
Then we say that

(f_{\varepsilon})\textit{ defines a generalized integrable map}:K\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

if

(i)

K\subseteq\langle\Omega_{\varepsilon}\rangle
and
[f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

for all
[x_{\varepsilon}]\in K
.

(ii)

\forall(x_{\varepsilon}),(x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}^{n}:\ [x_{\varepsilon}]=[x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]\in K\ \Rightarrow\ (f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}))\sim_{\rho}(f_{\varepsilon}(x^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}))
.

If
f\in\Set(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
is such that

\forall[x_{\varepsilon}]\in K:\ f\left([x_{\varepsilon}]\right)=\left[f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\right]

(7.3)

we say that
f:K\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is a
generalized
integrable function
.

We will again say that
f

is defined by the net

(f_{\varepsilon})
or that the net
(f_{\varepsilon})

represents

f
.
The set of all these generalized integrable functions will be denoted
by
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GI}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
.

E.g., if
f=[f_{\varepsilon}(-)]|_{K}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
, then
both
f
and
|f|=[|f_{\varepsilon}(-)|]|_{K}
are integrable on
K
.

As in Lemma
18
, we may assume without loss
of generality that
f_{\varepsilon}
are continuous maps defined on the
whole of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Theorem 58
.

Let
K\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

be
\mu
-measurable.

(i)

The definition of
\mu(K)
is independent of
the representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
.

(ii)

There exists a representative
(K_{\varepsilon})

of
K
such that
\mu(K)=[\mu(K_{\varepsilon})]
.

(iii)

Let
(K_{\varepsilon})
be any representative
of
K
and let
f=[f_{\varepsilon}(-)]|_{K}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GI}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
. Then

\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}:=\lim_{m\to\infty}\biggl[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\biggr]

exists and its value is independent of the representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
.

(iv)

There exists a representative
(K_{\varepsilon})

of
K
such that

\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}=\biggl[\int_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\biggr]

(7.4)

for each
f=[f_{\varepsilon}(-)]|_{K}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GI}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
.

(v)

If (
7.4
) holds, then
the same holds for any representative
(L_{\varepsilon})
of
K
with
L_{\varepsilon}\supseteq K_{\varepsilon}
,

\forall^{0}\varepsilon
.

Proof.

(i)
Let
(L_{\varepsilon})
be another representative.
As
[K_{\varepsilon}]\subseteq[L_{\varepsilon}]
, we have that
(\sup_{x\in K_{\varepsilon}}d(x,L_{\varepsilon}))_{\varepsilon}=:(n_{\varepsilon})

is negligible, so
K_{\varepsilon}\subseteq\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{n_{\varepsilon}}(L_{\varepsilon})
,
and
\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}))\leq\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m-1}}(L_{\varepsilon}))
.
Also using this inequality with the roles of
K_{\varepsilon}
and
L_{\varepsilon}

interchanged, we see that
\lim_{m\to\infty}[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon}))]

exists and that it equals
\lim_{m\to\infty}[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}))]
.

(ii)
Call
\left[c_{\varepsilon}\right]:=\mu(K)
and let

K=[L_{\varepsilon}]
. By definition of
\mu
-measurable set and by the
previous point
(i)
, for any
q\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists

m_{q}\in\mathbb{N}
(w.l.o.g.
m_{q}\geq q
) and
\varepsilon_{q}>0
(w.l.o.g.
\varepsilon_{q}<\varepsilon_{q-1}

and
\varepsilon_{q}<1/q
) such that

|\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m_{q}}}(L_{\varepsilon}))-c_{\varepsilon}|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q},\qquad\forall\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{q}

Now let
q_{\varepsilon}:=q
if
\varepsilon\in(\varepsilon_{q+1},\varepsilon_{q}]
. Then
q_{\varepsilon}\to\infty

as
\varepsilon\to 0
and

[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m_{q_{\varepsilon}}}}(L_{\varepsilon}))]=[c_{\varepsilon}]=\mu(K).

As also
(\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m_{q_{\varepsilon}}})
is negligible, we have
K=[\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m_{q_{\varepsilon}}}}(L_{\varepsilon})]

and hence the conclusion follows for
K_{\varepsilon}:=\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m_{q_{\varepsilon}}}}(L_{\varepsilon})
.

(iii)
–
(iv)
. Choose a representative

(K_{\varepsilon})
as in part
(ii)
. Then

\left|\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}-\int_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\right|\leq\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})\setminus K_{\varepsilon})\sup_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})}|f_{\varepsilon}|.

As
[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})\setminus K_{\varepsilon})]=[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}))]-[\mu(K_{\varepsilon})]\to 0

as
m\to\infty
and since
(\sup_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})}|f_{\varepsilon}|)

is moderate for some
m
and decreasing in
m
, we find that

\lim_{m\to\infty}\left[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\right]=\left[\int_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\right]

exists. Independence of the representative of
K
follows as in part

(i)
, if
f_{\varepsilon}\geq 0
. The general case follows
by considering the positive and negative part of
f_{\varepsilon}
.

(v)
Let
f_{\varepsilon}\geq 0
. Then by assumption,

[\int_{K_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}]\leq[\int_{L_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}]
.
For the converse inequality, observe that
[\int_{L_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}]\leq[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon})}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}]

for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Again the general case follows by considering
the positive and negative part of
f_{\varepsilon}
.
∎

The following Lemma provides an alternative characterization of
\mu
-measurability:

Lemma 59
.

A functionally compact set
K
is

\mu
-measurable if and only if there exists a representative
(K_{\varepsilon})

of
K
such that
[\mu(K_{\varepsilon})]=[\mu(L_{\varepsilon})]
, for each representative

(L_{\varepsilon})
of
K
with
L_{\varepsilon}\supseteq K_{\varepsilon}
,
\forall^{0}\varepsilon
.

Proof.

\Rightarrow
: by the previous Thm.
58
.

\Leftarrow
: it suffices to show that
\lim_{m\to\infty}[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}))]=[\mu(K_{\varepsilon})]
.
Seeking a contradiction, suppose that there exists
q\in\mathbb{N}
for which
it does not hold that

\exists M\,\forall m\geq M\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\ |\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}))-\mu(K_{\varepsilon})|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}.

Then we can construct a strictly increasing sequence
(m_{k})_{k}\to\infty

and a strictly decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{k})_{k}\to 0
such that

|\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon_{k}}^{m_{k}}}(K_{\varepsilon_{k}}))-\mu(K_{\varepsilon_{k}})|>\rho_{\varepsilon_{k}}^{q}
,

\forall k
.

Let
L_{\varepsilon}:=\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m_{k}}}(K_{\varepsilon})
,
whenever
\varepsilon\in(\varepsilon_{k+1},\varepsilon_{k}]
,
\forall k
. Then
K=[L_{\varepsilon}]
,
but
[\mu(K_{\varepsilon})]\neq[\mu(L_{\varepsilon})]
, as
|\mu(L_{\varepsilon})-\mu(K_{\varepsilon})|>\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}
,
for each
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{k}
(
k\in\mathbb{N}
).
∎

Example 60
.

Let
\lambda
denote the Lebesgue-measure.

(i)

If
K=\prod_{i=1}^{n}[a_{i},b_{i}]
, then
K
is
\lambda
-measurable
with

\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}=\left[\int_{a_{1,\varepsilon}}^{b_{1,\varepsilon}}\,dx_{1}\dots\int_{a_{n,\varepsilon}}^{b_{n,\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x_{n}$}}\right]

for any representatives
(a_{i,\varepsilon})
,
(b_{i,\varepsilon})
of
a_{i}

and
b_{i}
, respectively.

(ii)

Let
\rho_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon
, and

{\textstyle K:=\left\{\frac{1}{n}\mid n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}\right\}\cup\{0\}.}

Then
[K]
is
\lambda
-measurable with
\lambda([K])=0
. Indeed,
the contribution of
\{1/n\mid n>\varepsilon^{-m/2}\}
to
\lambda(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\varepsilon^{m}}(K))

is at most
\varepsilon^{m/2}+2\varepsilon^{m}
, while the contribution of
\{1/n\mid n\leq\varepsilon^{-m/2}\}

is at most
2\varepsilon^{m}\varepsilon^{-m/2}=2\varepsilon^{m/2}
. Thus
\lim_{m\to\infty}[\lambda(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\varepsilon^{m}}(K))]=0
.

(iii)

Let
\rho_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon
, and

K:={\textstyle\left\{\frac{1}{\log n}\mid n\in\mathbb{N}_{>1}\right\}\cup\{0\}.}

Then
[K]
is not
\lambda
-measurable. For, if
n\geq\frac{\varepsilon^{-m}}{(\log\varepsilon^{-m})^{2}}
,
then, by the mean value theorem,

\frac{1}{\log n}-\frac{1}{\log(n+1)}\leq\frac{1}{n(\log n)^{2}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{m}(\log\varepsilon^{-m})^{2}}{\Bigl(\log\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon^{-m}}{(\log\varepsilon^{-m})^{2}}\Bigr)\Bigr)^{2}}\leq 2\varepsilon^{m}

for small
\varepsilon
. So the contribution of
\left\{\frac{1}{\log n}\mid n\geq\frac{\varepsilon^{-m}}{(\log\varepsilon^{-m})^{2}}\right\}

to
\lambda(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\varepsilon^{m}}(K))
lies between
\frac{1}{\log(\varepsilon^{-m})}

and
\frac{2}{\log(\varepsilon^{-m})}
for small
\varepsilon
. The contribution
of
\Bigl\{\frac{1}{\log n}\mid n<\frac{\varepsilon^{-m}}{(\log\varepsilon^{-m})^{2}}\Bigr\}

to
\lambda(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\varepsilon^{m}}(K))
is at most
\frac{2}{(\log\varepsilon^{-m})^{2}}
,
which is of a lower order. Thus
\lim_{m\to\infty}[\lambda(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\varepsilon^{m}}(K))]

does not exist.

7.2.
Hyperfinite limits

We start by defining the set of hypernatural numbers in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

and the set of
\rho
-moderate nets of natural numbers. For a deeper
study of these notions, see
[
82
]
.

Definition 61
.

We set

(i)

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}:=\left\{[n_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid n_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}\quad\forall\varepsilon\right\}

(ii)

\mathbb{N}_{\rho}:=\left\{(n_{\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}\mid n_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}\quad\forall\varepsilon\right\}.

Therefore,
n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
if and only if there exists

(x_{\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}
such that
n=[\text{int}(|x_{\varepsilon}|)]
.
Clearly,
\mathbb{N}\subset\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
. Note that the integer part function

\text{int}(-)
is not well-defined on
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. In fact, if
x=1=\left[1-\rho_{\varepsilon}^{1/\varepsilon}\right]=\left[1+\rho_{\varepsilon}^{1/\varepsilon}\right]
,
then
\text{int}\left(1-\rho_{\varepsilon}^{1/\varepsilon}\right)=0
, whereas
\text{int}\left(1+\rho_{\varepsilon}^{1/\varepsilon}\right)=1
,
for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small. Similar counterexamples can be constructed
for floor and ceiling functions.

However, the nearest integer function is well defined on

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
.

Lemma 62
.

Let
(n_{\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{N}_{\rho}
and
(x_{\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}

be such that
[n_{\varepsilon}]=[x_{\varepsilon}]
. Let
\text{\emph{rpi}}:\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}

be the function rounding to the nearest integer with tie breaking
towards positive infinity. Then
\text{\emph{rpi}}(x_{\varepsilon})=n_{\varepsilon}

for
\varepsilon
small. The same result holds using
\text{\emph{rni}}:\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
the function rounding half towards
-\infty
.

Proof.

We have
\text{rpi}(x)=\lfloor x+\frac{1}{2}\rfloor
, where
\lfloor-\rfloor

is the floor function. For
\varepsilon
small,
\rho_{\varepsilon}<\frac{1}{2}

and, since
[n_{\varepsilon}]=[x_{\varepsilon}]
, for such
\varepsilon
we can also
have
n_{\varepsilon}-\rho_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}<x_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}<n_{\varepsilon}+\rho_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}
.
But
n_{\varepsilon}\leq n_{\varepsilon}-\rho_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}
and
n_{\varepsilon}+\rho_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}<n_{\varepsilon}+1
.
Therefore
\lfloor x_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\rfloor=n_{\varepsilon}
. An analogous
argument can be applied to
\text{rni}(-)
.
∎

Actually, this lemma does not allow us to define a
nearest
integer
function
\text{ni}{}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}_{\rho}
as
\text{ni}{([x_{\varepsilon}])}:=\text{rpi}(x_{\varepsilon})

because if
[x_{\varepsilon}]=[n_{\varepsilon}]
, the equality
n_{\varepsilon}=\text{rpi}(x_{\varepsilon})

holds only for
\varepsilon
small. We should therefore consider the function

ni
as valued in the germs for
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
generated by
nets in
\mathbb{N}_{\rho}
. A simpler approach is to choose a representative

(n_{\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{N}_{\rho}
for each
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
and to define

\text{ni}{(x)}:=(n_{\varepsilon})
. Clearly, we must consider the net
\left(\text{ni}{(x)}_{\varepsilon}\right)

only for
\varepsilon
small, such as in equalities of the form
x=\left[\text{ni}{(x)}_{\varepsilon}\right]
.
This is what we do in the following

Definition 63
.

The nearest integer function
\text{ni}(-)
is defined
by:

(i)

\text{ni}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}:\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}_{\rho}

(ii)

If
[x_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
and
\text{ni}\left([x_{\varepsilon}]\right)=(n_{\varepsilon})

then
\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\ n_{\varepsilon}=\text{rpi}(x_{\varepsilon})
.

In other words, if
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
, then
x=\left[\text{ni}(x)_{\varepsilon}\right]

and
\text{ni}(x)_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
for all
\varepsilon
.

We first consider the notion of hyperlimit. As we will see clearly
in Example
66
.
(i)
, a key point
in the definition of hyperlimit is to consider
two
gauges.
This is a natural way of proceeding because different gauges define
different topologies. On the other hand, the notion of hyperlimit
corresponds exactly to that of limit in the sharp topology on
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

of a generalized sequence (hypersequence), i.e. defined on the directed
set
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
.

Definition 64
.

Let
\rho
,
\sigma
be two gauges (see Def.
1
).
Let
(a_{n})_{n}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
be a
\sigma
-hypersequence
of
\rho
-generalized numbers. Finally let
l\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. Then we
say that

l\text{ is the hyperlimit of }(a_{n})_{n}

if

\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\,\exists M\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,\forall n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}:\ n\geq M\ \Rightarrow\ \left|a_{n}-l\right|<\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}.

(7.5)

Remark 65
.

(i)

In a hyperlimit, we are considering
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
as an ordered
set directed by
\leq
:

n,m\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\ \Rightarrow\ n\vee m=\left[\max\left(\text{ni}(n)_{\varepsilon},\text{ni}(m)_{\varepsilon}\right)\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}.

On the other hand, on
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
we are considering the sharp topology
(which is Hausdorff). In fact, if
l
,
\lambda
are hyperlimits
of
a:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, then

\left|l-\lambda\right|\leq\left|l-a_{M}\right|+\left|a_{M}-\lambda\right|\leq 2\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q+1}<\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}

for all
q
. So
l=\lambda\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. We will therefore use the
notations

l=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}a_{n}

or simply
l=\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}a_{n}
if
\sigma=\rho
.

(ii)

A sufficient condition to extend an ordinary
sequence
a:\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
of
\rho
-generalized numbers to the whole
of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
is

\forall n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}:\ \left(a_{\text{ni}(n)_{\varepsilon}}\right)\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}.

(7.6)

In fact, in this way
a_{n}
is well-defined because of Lem.
62
;
on the other hand, using (
7.6
), we have defined an extension
of the old sequence
a
because if
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then
\text{ni}(n)_{\varepsilon}=n

for
\varepsilon
small and hence we get
a_{n}=[a_{n}]
. For example,
the sequence of infinities
a_{n}=\frac{1}{n}+\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}
for
all
n\in\mathbb{N}
can be extended to any
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
, whereas
a_{n}=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\sigma}{\hbox{\rm d}\sigma}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\sigma$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\sigma$}}^{-n}

can be extended as
a:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
only for certain
gauges
\rho
, e.g. if the gauges satisfy

\exists N\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\ \sigma_{\varepsilon}^{n}\geq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{N},

e.g.
\sigma_{\varepsilon}\geq-\log(\rho_{\varepsilon})^{-1}
.

Example 66
.

(i)

The following example strongly motivates the
use of two gauges. Let
\rho
be a gauge and set
\sigma_{\varepsilon}:=\exp\left(-\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}}}\right)
,
so that also
\sigma
is a gauge. We have

\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{1}{\log n}=0\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\quad\text{whereas}\quad\not\exists\,\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{1}{\log n}.

In fact, if
n>1
, we have
0<\frac{1}{\log n}<\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}

if and only if
\log n>\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-q}
, i.e.
n>e^{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-q}}

(in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
). We can thus take
M:=\left[\text{int}\left(e^{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-q}}\right)+1\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}

because
e^{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-q}}<\exp\left(\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}}}\right)=\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{-1}

for
\varepsilon
small.

Vice versa, by contradiction, if
\exists\,\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{1}{\log n}=:l\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
then by the definition of hyperlimit from
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
to
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

we would get the existence of
M\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
such that

\forall n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}:\ n\geq M\ \Rightarrow\ \frac{1}{\log n}-\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}<l<\frac{1}{\log n}+\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}.

(7.7)

Since
M
is
\rho
-moderate, we always have
0<\frac{1}{\log M}-\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}
,
so
l>0
. Thus
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{p}<|l|
for some
p\in\mathbb{N}
. Setting

q:=\min\left\{p\in\mathbb{N}\mid\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{p}<|l|\right\}+1,

we get that
\left|l_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}\right|<\rho_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{q}

for some sequence
(\bar{\varepsilon}_{k})_{k}\downarrow 0
. Therefore

\frac{1}{\log M_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}}<l_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}+\rho_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}\leq\left|l_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}\right|+\rho_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}<\rho_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{q}+\rho_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}

and hence
M_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}>\exp\left(\frac{1}{\rho_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}^{q}+\rho_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{k}}}\right)

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, which is in contradiction with
M\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
because

q\geq 1
.

(ii)

For all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
, we have
\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}\frac{1}{n^{k}}=0
.
In fact, for all
n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
, we have
0<\frac{1}{n^{k}}<\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{q}

if and only if
n^{k}>\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-q}
, i.e.
n>\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-\frac{q}{k}}
.
Thus, it suffices to take
M_{\varepsilon}:=\text{int}\left(\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{q}{k}}\right)+1

in the definition of hyperlimit. Analogously, we can treat rational
functions having degree of denominator greater or equal to that of
the numerator.

7.3.
Properties of multidimensional integral

We start by proving the change of variable formula.

Lemma 67
.

Let
K=[K_{\varepsilon}]
be functionally
compact and
\varphi=[\varphi_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
with
\det(d\varphi)(x)

invertible for each
x\in K
. If
\varphi
is injective on
K
, then
the
\varphi_{\varepsilon}
are injective on
K_{\varepsilon}
,
\forall^{0}\varepsilon
.

Proof.

By contradiction, suppose that for each
\eta>0
, there exists
\varepsilon<\eta

such that
\varphi_{\varepsilon}
is not injective on
K_{\varepsilon}
. Then we
find for such
\varepsilon
some
x_{\varepsilon}
,
y_{\varepsilon}\in K_{\varepsilon}
with

x_{\varepsilon}\neq y_{\varepsilon}
and
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})=\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon})
.
For all other
\varepsilon
, define
x_{\varepsilon}=y_{\varepsilon}\in K_{\varepsilon}
arbitrary.
Then
x:=[x_{\varepsilon}]
,
y:=[y_{\varepsilon}]\in K
and
\varphi(x)=\varphi(y)
.
As
\varphi
is injective,
x=y
. But then this contradicts the local
injectivity of
\varphi_{\varepsilon}
on
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{r_{\varepsilon}}(x_{\varepsilon})
for
some
[r_{\varepsilon}]>0
, see also
[
41
, Thm. 6]
and
[
26
]
.
∎

Theorem 68
.

Let
K\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be
\lambda
-measurable,
where
\lambda
is the Lebesgue measure, and let
\varphi\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})

be such that
\varphi^{-1}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\varphi(K),\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
. Then
\varphi(K)

is
\lambda
-measurable and

\int_{\varphi(K)}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}=\int_{K}(f\circ\varphi)\left|\det(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi}{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi$}})\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}

for each
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\varphi(K),\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
.

Proof.

Let
x\in\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})
. Then there
exists
y\in K_{\varepsilon}
such that
|x-y|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}
. As
\varphi\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
,

|\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)-\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y)|\leq|x-y|\sup_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})}\|\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}}{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}$}}\|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m-M}

for some
M\in\mathbb{N}
(not depending on
m
). Thus
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}))\subseteq\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m-M}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(K_{\varepsilon}))
.
Applying this to
\varphi^{-1}
, we find that also
\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(K_{\varepsilon}))\subseteq\varphi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m-M}}(K_{\varepsilon}))

for some
M\in\mathbb{N}
. Now let
f_{\varepsilon}\geq 0
. As
(\det(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{-1}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}^{-1}$}}))

is moderate,
|\det(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}}{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}$}})(x)|>0
for each
x\in K
.
Thus by Lem.
67
, w.l.o.g
\varphi_{\varepsilon}

are injective. Then

\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(K_{\varepsilon}))}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}\leq\int_{\varphi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m-M}}(K_{\varepsilon}))}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}=\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m-M}}(K_{\varepsilon})}(f_{\varepsilon}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon})|\det\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}}{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}$}}|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}

and

\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m+M}}(K_{\varepsilon})}(f_{\varepsilon}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon})|\det\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}}{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}$}}|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}=\int_{\varphi_{\varepsilon}(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m+M}}(K_{\varepsilon}))}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}\leq\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(K_{\varepsilon}))}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}

Since
\lim_{m\to\infty}\bigl[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m-M}}(K_{\varepsilon})}(f_{\varepsilon}\circ\varphi_{\varepsilon})|\det\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}}{\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\varphi_{\varepsilon}$}}|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}\bigr]

exists, it follows from the previous inequalities that also
\lim_{m\to\infty}\bigl[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}(K_{\varepsilon}))}f_{\varepsilon}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\hbox{\rm d}\lambda}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\lambda$}}\bigr]

exists, with the same value. The general case follows by considering
the positive and negative part of
f_{\varepsilon}
.
∎

We now consider the problem of additivity of the integral.

Definition 69
.

Let
K
,
L
be functionally compact.
Then we call
K
and
L

strongly disjoint
if the following
equivalent conditions hold:

(i)

for each representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
of
K
and
(L_{\varepsilon})
of

L
,
K_{\varepsilon}\cap L_{\varepsilon}=\emptyset
,
\forall^{0}\varepsilon

(ii)

for some (and thus each) representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
of
K
and

(L_{\varepsilon})
of
L
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})\cap\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon})=\emptyset
,

\forall^{0}\varepsilon

(iii)

\forall e\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}:\ e^{2}=e,e\neq 0\ \Rightarrow\ Ke\cap Le=\emptyset

(iv)

\forall H\subseteq_{0}I:\ K|_{H}\cap L|_{H}=\emptyset

(v)

x\neq y
for each subpoint
x
of
K
and
y
of
L
.

Definition 70
.

Let
K
,
L
be functionally
compact. Then we call
K
and
L

almost strongly disjoint

if the following equivalent conditions hold:

(i)

for each representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
of
K
and
(L_{\varepsilon})
of

L
,
[\mu(K_{\varepsilon}\cap L_{\varepsilon})]=0

(ii)

for some (and thus each) representative
(K_{\varepsilon})
of
K
and

(L_{\varepsilon})
of
L

\lim_{m\to\infty}[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})\cap\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon}))]=0.

The equivalence of the conditions follows by a similar argument
as in Lem.
59
, e.g.:

Lemma 71
.

The conditions in Def.
70
are equivalent.

Proof.

(i)\Rightarrow(ii)
: let
K=[K_{\varepsilon}]
and
L=[L_{\varepsilon}]
. Seeking
a contradiction, suppose that there exists
q\in\mathbb{N}
for which it
does not hold that

\exists M\,\forall m\geq M\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\ \mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})\cap\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon}))\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}.

Then we can construct a strictly increasing sequence
(m_{k})_{k}\to\infty

and a strictly decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{k})_{k}\to 0
s.t.
\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon_{k}}^{m_{k}}}(K_{\varepsilon_{k}})\cap\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon_{k}}^{m_{k}}}(L_{\varepsilon_{k}}))>\rho_{\varepsilon_{k}}^{q}
,

\forall k
.

Let
K^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}:=\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m_{k}}}(K_{\varepsilon})

and
L^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}:=\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m_{k}}}(L_{\varepsilon})
,
whenever
\varepsilon\in(\varepsilon_{k+1},\varepsilon_{k}]
,
\forall k
. Then
K=[K^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]

and
L=[L^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
, but
[\mu(K^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\cap L^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})]\neq 0
, as

\mu(K^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\cap L^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})>\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}
, for each
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{k}

(
k\in\mathbb{N}
).

(ii)\Rightarrow(i)
: let
(K_{\varepsilon})
,
(L_{\varepsilon})
as in (ii),
and
K=[K^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
and
L=[L^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
. For each
q\in\mathbb{N}
, we
have that

[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})\cap\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon}))]\leq\rho^{q}

for sufficiently large
m\in\mathbb{N}
. As
[K^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]\subseteq[K_{\varepsilon}]
,

K^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\subseteq\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})
,

\forall^{0}\varepsilon
, and similarly for
L
, and thus also
[\mu(K^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\cap L^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})]\leq\rho^{q}
.
∎

E.g., if
a\leq b\leq c
, then
[a,b]
and
[b,c]
are
almost strongly disjoint. Obviously, strongly disjoint sets are almost
strongly disjoint. Recall that the union of two internal sets is usually
not internal, but

K\vee L:=[K_{\varepsilon}\cup L_{\varepsilon}]=\{e_{S}x+e_{{S}^{c}}y:x\in K,y\in L,S\subseteq\left]0,1\right]\}

is the smallest internal set containing
K
and
L

[
86
]
.
E.g.,
[a,b]\vee[b,c]=[a,c]
.

Theorem 72
.

If
K
,
L
are almost strongly disjoint
\mu
-measurable subsets
of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, then
K\vee L
is also
\mu
-measurable and
for each
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GI}(K\vee L,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

\int_{K\vee L}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}=\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}+\int_{L}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}.

Proof.

As
\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}\cup L_{\varepsilon})=\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})\cup\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon})
,

\displaystyle\left[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon}\cup L_{\varepsilon})}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\right]+\left[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon})}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\right]-

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\left[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})\cap\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon})}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\right].

Since

\displaystyle\left|\left[\int_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})\cap\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon})}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left[\mu(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})\cap\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon}))\right]\cdot

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq}\cdot\left[\sup_{\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(K_{\varepsilon})\cap\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}(L_{\varepsilon})}|f|\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m\to\infty}}{{\to}}0

we see that
K\vee L
is
\mu
-measurable with
\int_{K\vee L}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}=\int_{K}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}+\int_{L}f\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}
.
∎

Example 73
.

Let
S\subseteq[0,1[
with
0\in\overline{S}
and
0\in\overline{{S}^{c}}
.
Let
K_{\varepsilon}=\begin{cases}[0,1],&\varepsilon\in S\\
{}[0,3],&\varepsilon\in{S}^{c}\end{cases}
and
L_{\varepsilon}=\begin{cases}[2,3],&\varepsilon\in S\\
{}[0,3],&\varepsilon\in{S}^{c}.\end{cases}
Then
K\cap L=\emptyset
and
\mu(K\vee L)=2e_{S}+3e_{{S}^{c}}\neq 2e_{S}+6e_{{S}^{c}}=\mu(K)+\mu(L)
.
Thus the condition that
K
and
L
are almost strongly disjoint
cannot be replaced by the condition that
K\cap L=\emptyset
.

Theorem 74
.

Let
K\Subset_{\text{f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Let
f_{n}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GI}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
,

\forall n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
. If
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}f_{n}(x)

exists for each
x\in K
, then the convergence is uniform over
K

and the limit function is integrable on
K
.

Proof.

We first show that the sequence is uniformly Cauchy, i.e. that for
each
m\in\mathbb{N}

\exists N\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall k,l\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}},k,l\geq\sigma^{-N}\,\forall x\in K:|f_{k}(x)-f_{l}(x)|\leq\rho^{m}.

(7.8)

Seeking a contradiction, suppose that for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\forall N\in\mathbb{N}\,\exists k,l\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}},k,l\geq\sigma^{-N}\,\exists x\in K:\ |f_{k}(x)-f_{l}(x)|\not\leq\rho^{m}.

We thus construct sequences
(k_{N})_{N}
and
(l_{N})_{N}
in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
,
with
k_{N},l_{N}\geq\sigma^{-N}
for which there exist
x_{N}\in K

s.t.
|f_{k_{N}}(x_{N})-f_{l_{N}}(x_{N})|\not\leq\rho^{m}
,
\forall N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
K=[K_{\varepsilon}]
and
f_{k}=[f_{k,\varepsilon}]
. We thus find
S_{N}\subseteq(0,1]

with
0\in\overline{S_{N}}
such that
|f_{k_{N},\varepsilon}(x_{N,\varepsilon})-f_{l_{N},\varepsilon}(x_{N,\varepsilon})|\geq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}

for each
\varepsilon\in S_{N}
. Then choose a decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n}\to 0

such that

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}\in S_{1}

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{2}\in S_{2};\varepsilon_{3}\in S_{1};

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{4}\in S_{3};\varepsilon_{5}\in S_{2};\varepsilon_{6}\in S_{1};

\displaystyle\dots

Let
x_{\varepsilon_{n}}:=x_{N,\varepsilon_{n}}
if
\varepsilon_{n}\in S_{N}
, for each

n\in\mathbb{N}
. Extend to a net
(x_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}
with
x_{\varepsilon}\in K_{\varepsilon}
,

\forall\varepsilon
. Then
xe_{U_{N}}=x_{N}e_{U_{N}}
for some
U_{N}\subseteq S_{N}

with
0\in\overline{U_{N}}
, and therefore
|f_{k_{N}}(x)-f_{l_{N}}(x)|e_{U_{N}}\geq\rho^{m}e_{U_{N}}
,

\forall N
. We thus contradict the fact that
(f_{n}(x))_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}}

is a convergent hypersequence.

By taking the limit for
l\to\infty
in (
7.8
),
we conclude that
(f_{n})_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}}
is uniformly convergent
on
K
.

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, fix a representative
(f_{\sigma^{-n},\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}

of
f_{\sigma^{-n}}
with
\sup_{x\in K_{\varepsilon}}|f_{\sigma^{-n},\varepsilon}|\leq\varepsilon^{-M}
,

\forall\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,
\forall n\in\mathbb{N}
(some
M\in\mathbb{N}
, independent
of
n
). For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
N_{m}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\forall n,n^{\prime}\geq N_{m}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\sup_{x\in K_{\varepsilon}}|f_{\sigma^{-n},\varepsilon}(x)-f_{\sigma^{-n^{\prime}},\varepsilon}(x)|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}.

Then for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists some
\varepsilon_{k}>0
such that

\forall\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{k}\,\forall m\leq k\,\forall n,n^{\prime}\in[N_{m},k]:\sup_{x\in K_{\varepsilon}}|f_{\sigma^{-n},\varepsilon}(x)-f_{\sigma^{-n^{\prime}},\varepsilon}(x)|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}.

W.l.o.g.,
(\varepsilon_{k})_{k}\downarrow 0
. Let
n_{\varepsilon}:=k
, for
\varepsilon\in(\varepsilon_{k+1},\varepsilon_{k}]
.
Then

\forall m\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N},n\geq N_{m}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\sup_{x\in K_{\varepsilon}}|f_{\sigma^{-n},\varepsilon}(x)-f_{\sigma^{-n_{\varepsilon}},\varepsilon}(x)|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}.

Thus the limit function
f=[f_{\sigma^{-n_{\varepsilon}},\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GI}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
.
∎

Theorem 75
.

Let
K\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be
\mu
-measurable and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
.
If
