\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}f_{n}(x)
exists for each
x\in K
, then

\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}f_{n}
is integrable on
K
and

\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\int_{K}f_{n}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}=\int_{K}\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.42224pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}f_{n}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\hbox{\rm d}\mu}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\mu$}}.

Proof.

By Thm.
74
,
(f_{n})_{n}
uniformly converges
to some
f=[f_{\varepsilon}]
. Then

\left|\int_{K}f_{n}-\int_{K}f\right|\leq\int_{K}|f_{n}-f|\leq\rho^{m}\mu(K)

as soon as
n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
is large enough.
∎

8.
Sheaf properties

The aim of this section is to establish appropriate sheaf properties
for GSF. That this task is not entirely straightforward can be seen
from the following example, which can be easily reformulated in other
non-Archimedean settings:

Example 76
.

Let
i:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
be as in Rem.
40
,
i.e.,
i(x):=1
if
x\approx 0
and
i(x):=0
otherwise. The domain

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
of this function is the disjoint union of the sharply open
sets
D_{\infty}=\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid x\approx 0\}
and its complement

D_{\infty}^{\text{c}}
. Moreover,
i|_{D_{\infty}}\equiv 1
and

i|_{D_{\infty}^{\text{c}}}\equiv 0
are both GSF. However, as we
have seen in the remark following Cor.
48
,
i

itself is
not
a GSF. This shows that
{}^{\rho}\mathcal{G}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
is not
a sheaf with respect to the sharp topology.

Trivially, if we introduce the space of (sharply) locally
defined GSF by means of
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}_{\text{loc}}(X,Y)
if
f:X\to Y
,
and
\forall x\in X\,\exists r\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}:\ f|_{B_{r}(x)\cap X}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(B_{r}(x)\cap X,Y)
,
then
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}_{\text{loc}}(-,Y)
is naturally a sheaf with respect to
the sharp topology. By Example
76
, however,
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}_{\text{loc}}(X,Y)

is strictly larger than
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,Y)
. This fact can be viewed as a
necessary trade-off between the classical statement of locality for
generalized functions, on the one hand, and the requirement to preserve
classical theorems from smooth analysis on the other. In the above
example, it is the validity of an intermediate value theorem in our
setting that precludes the function
i
from qualifying as a GSF.
Conversely, it follows that this result does not hold in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}_{\text{loc}}(X,Y)
.
Any theory of generalized functions that is based on set-theoretical
functions and includes actual infinitesimals has to face these dichotomies
related to the total disconnectedness of its non-Archimedean ring
of scalars.

The general scheme of this section is:

(a)

We are searching for a new compatibility/coherence
condition for an arbitrarily indexed family
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J}

of GSF (throughout this section,
J
will be an arbitrary set), which
allows us to prove a corresponding sheaf property. We will call this
property
dynamic compatibility condition
(DCC).

(b)

The DCC must imply the classical one.
Note that for particular types of covers the classic coherence condition
may still work, e.g. covers made of near-standard points and large
open sets (see Cor.
87
below) or those made of
increasing sequences of internal sets (see Thm.
77

below).

(c)

The DCC must be a necessary condition
if we assume that the sections
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
glue
together into a GSF.

(d)

The sheaf property based on the DCC
should be a particular case of the general abstract notion of sheaf
(see Sec.
9
).

We start from the following sheaf property (originally proved in
[
115
]
):

Theorem 77
.

Let
X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
Y\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

and
K_{q}\Subset_{\text{f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
f_{q}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K_{q},Y)
for all
q\in\mathbb{N}
,
where
X=\bigcup_{q\in\mathbb{N}}K_{q}
,
K_{q}\subseteq\text{\emph{int}}\left(K_{q+1}\right)

and
f_{q+1}|_{K_{q}}=f_{q}
for each
q\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there exists
a unique
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,Y)
such that
f|_{K_{q}}=f_{q}
for all
q\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Let
f_{q}=[f_{q,\varepsilon}(-)]
and
K_{q}=[K_{q,\varepsilon}]
, for each
q\in\mathbb{N}
.
By Lem.
11
.
(v)
,
there exist
k_{q}\in\mathbb{N}
(
k_{q}
recursively chosen so that
(k_{q})_{q}

is increasing) such that
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{k_{q}}}(K_{q,\varepsilon})\subseteq K_{q+1,\varepsilon}
,
for each
q
,
\varepsilon
. We may assume
Y\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
(in general,
one can apply the one-dimensional case componentwise). Let
\theta\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

with
\theta(x)=0
, if
\left|x\right|\geq 1
and
\theta(x)\geq 0
, for each

x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\theta=1
and let
r\odot\theta_{r}(x):=r^{-n}\theta({r}^{-1}x)
,
for
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
. Let
1_{A}
denote the characteristic function
of a set
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and set

\varphi_{q,\varepsilon}:=1_{K_{q+3,\varepsilon}\setminus K_{q,\varepsilon}}*\rho_{\varepsilon}^{k_{q+3}}\odot\theta,\quad\forall q,\varepsilon.

If
y\in B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{k_{q+3}}}(x)\cap K_{q,\varepsilon}
, then
x\in B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{k_{q}}}(K_{q,\varepsilon})\subseteq K_{q+1,\varepsilon}
,
and hence
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{k_{q+3}}}(x)\cap K_{q,\varepsilon}=\emptyset

if
x\notin K_{q+1,\varepsilon}
. If
x\in K_{q+2,\varepsilon}
, then
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{k_{q+3}}}(x)\subseteq B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{k_{q+2}}}(K_{q+2,\varepsilon})\subseteq K_{q+3,\varepsilon}
.
Thereby,
\varphi_{q,\varepsilon}(x)=1
, for each
x\in K_{q+2,\varepsilon}\setminus K_{q+1,\varepsilon}
.
Moreover,
\mbox{stsupp}\varphi_{q,\varepsilon}\subseteq K_{q+3,\varepsilon}+B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{k_{q+3}}}(0)=B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{k_{q+3}}}(K_{q+3,\varepsilon})\subseteq K_{q+4,\varepsilon}
.
Further,
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\partial^{\alpha}\varphi_{q,\varepsilon}(x)\right|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-k_{q+3}\left|\alpha\right|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\partial^{\alpha}\theta\right|

by the properties of the convolution. Let
\varphi_{\varepsilon}:=\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}}\varphi_{q,\varepsilon}
.
Then
\varphi_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bigcup_{q\in\mathbb{N}}K_{q,\varepsilon})
and for
each
q
,
(\sup_{x\in K_{q,\varepsilon}}\left|\partial^{\alpha}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\right|)\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}
.
Also
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\geq 1
, for each
x\in\bigcup_{q\in\mathbb{N}}K_{q,\varepsilon}
.
Let
\psi_{q,\varepsilon}:=\varphi_{q,\varepsilon}/\varphi_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
Then
\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}}\psi_{q,\varepsilon}(x)=1
, for each
x\in\bigcup_{q\in\mathbb{N}}K_{q,\varepsilon}
.
Since
\sup_{x\in K_{q,\varepsilon}}\left|1/\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)\right|\leq 1
, we find
that
(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\partial^{\alpha}\psi_{q,\varepsilon}(x)\right|)\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}
,
for each
q
. Let
f_{\varepsilon}:=\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}}\psi_{q,\varepsilon}\cdot f_{q+3,\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bigcup_{q\in\mathbb{N}}K_{q,\varepsilon})
,
for each
\varepsilon
(recall that
\mbox{stsupp}\psi_{q,\varepsilon}\subseteq K_{q+3,\varepsilon}
).
Then for each
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
and
x=[x_{\varepsilon}]\in K_{N}

(without loss of generality,
x_{\varepsilon}\in K_{N,\varepsilon}
, for each
\varepsilon
),

\left|\partial^{\alpha}f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\right|\leq\sum_{q\leq N+3}\left|\partial^{\alpha}(\psi_{q,\varepsilon}\cdot f_{q+3,\varepsilon})(x_{\varepsilon})\right|\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}

and

\left|f_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})-f_{N,\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\right|\leq\sum_{q\leq N+3}\left|\psi_{q,\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\right|\left|f_{q+3,\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})-f_{N,\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\right|\sim_{\rho}0

since
\mbox{stsupp}\psi_{q,\varepsilon}\subseteq K_{q+3,\varepsilon}
. Hence
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,Y)

with
{{f}|_{K_{N}}}=f_{N}
.
∎

This property allows us to firstly prove a sheaf property
for
K=[K_{\varepsilon}]\Subset_{\text{f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and secondly to use Thm.
77

to extend it to domains
X
that satisfy the previous assumptions,
i.e. the following

Definition 78
.

Let
X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, then we say that

X

admits a functionally compact exhaustion
if there exists
a sequence
(K_{q})_{q\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

(i)

K_{q}\Subset_{\text{f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
;

(ii)

K_{q}\subseteq\text{int}\left(K_{q+1}\right)
;

(iii)

X=\bigcup_{q\in\mathbb{N}}K_{q}
.

For example, every strongly internal set
X=\langle A_{\varepsilon}\rangle

admits a functionally compact exhaustion since we can consider

\displaystyle K_{q\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-q}}(0)\cap\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{-\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}}(A_{\varepsilon})\qquad\forall q\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle K_{q}

\displaystyle:=\left[{K_{q\varepsilon}}\right]\Subset_{\text{f}}X

(8.1)

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{q\in\mathbb{N}}K_{q}

where
\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{-r}(A):=\{x\in A\mid d(x,A^{c})\geq r\}
.
Other simple examples are e.g. the intervals
(0,a]
or
(-\infty,a]
.

8.1.
The Lebesgue generalized number

We first introduce a notation for a specified Lebesgue number:

Lemma 79
.

Let
K\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
(V_{j})_{j\in J}
be
an open cover of
K
. For
x\in K
, set

\displaystyle\sigma(x)

\displaystyle:=\sup\{r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}\mid\exists j\in J:\ B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{r}(x)\subseteq V_{j}\}

(8.2)

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\min\{\sigma(x)\mid x\in K\};\text{ if }K=\emptyset,\text{ set }\sigma:=1.

Then
\sigma(-):K\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}_{>0}
is a continuous function and
\sigma

is a Lebesgue number of
(V_{j})_{j\in J}
for
K
, i.e.

\forall x\in K\,\exists j\in J:\ B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{\sigma}(x)\subseteq V_{j}.

(8.3)

We use the notation
\text{\emph{Lebnum}}\left((V_{j})_{j\in J},K\right)=:\sigma
.

Proof.

See the proof of
[
12
, Thm. 1.6.11]
.
∎

Lemma 80
.

Let
K=[K_{\varepsilon}]\Subset_{\text{f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
with
K_{\varepsilon}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}

for all
\varepsilon
. Assume that

K\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j},

(8.4)

where
U_{j}=\langle U_{j\varepsilon}\rangle
are strongly internal sets, and set

\displaystyle s_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=\text{\emph{Lebnum}}\left(\left(U_{j\varepsilon}\right)_{j\in J},K_{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle:=[s_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}.

Then
s>0
.

Proof.

By contradiction, assume that
s\not>0
, so that
s_{\varepsilon_{k}}<\rho_{\varepsilon_{k}}^{k}

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and for some sequence
(\varepsilon_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\downarrow 0
.
By definition of
Lebnum
we can write
s_{\varepsilon_{k}}=\frac{1}{2}\sigma(x_{\varepsilon_{k}})<\rho_{\varepsilon_{k}}^{k}

for some
x_{\varepsilon_{k}}\in K_{\varepsilon_{k}}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
. By (
8.2
)
we hence have

\forall k\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall j\in J:\ B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{2\rho_{\varepsilon_{k}}^{k}}(c_{\varepsilon_{k}})\not\subseteq U_{j\varepsilon_{k}}.

(8.5)

Note that the conclusion is trivial if
K_{\varepsilon}=\emptyset
for
\varepsilon

small. We can therefore assume that there exists some
h_{\varepsilon}\in K_{\varepsilon}

for all
\varepsilon
. Let
x_{\varepsilon}:=x_{\varepsilon_{k}}
if
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{k}

and
x_{\varepsilon}:=h_{\varepsilon}
otherwise, so that
x_{\varepsilon}\in K_{\varepsilon}\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j\varepsilon}

for small
\varepsilon
(see Lem.
11
.
(i)

and recall that
K\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}\subseteq\langle\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j\varepsilon}\rangle
).
Thereby,
x:=[x_{\varepsilon}]\in K\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}
. So,

x\in U_{j}
for some
j\in J
, and hence
\overline{B_{R}(x)}\subseteq U_{j}=\langle U_{j\varepsilon}\rangle

for some
R\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, so that
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{R_{\varepsilon_{k}}}(x_{\varepsilon_{k}})\subseteq U_{j\varepsilon_{k}}

for
k\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large by Lem.
11
.
(i)

and (
2.6
). Since also
2\rho_{\varepsilon_{k}}^{k}<R_{\varepsilon_{k}}

for
k\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large, we can finally say that
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{2\rho_{\varepsilon_{k}}^{k}}(x_{\varepsilon_{k}})\subseteq B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{R_{\varepsilon_{k}}}(x_{\varepsilon_{k}})\subseteq U_{j\varepsilon_{k}}
,
which contradicts (
8.5
).
∎

On the basis of this result, we can set

\text{Lebnum}\left(\left(U_{j}\right)_{j\in J},K\right)=:s\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}.

Assumption (
8.4
) cannot be replaced by the weaker
K\subseteq\langle\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j\varepsilon}\rangle
:
let
K_{\varepsilon}:=[-1,1]_{\mathbb{R}}
,
J:=\{1,2\}
,
c_{1\varepsilon}:=-1
,
c_{2\varepsilon}:=1
,

r_{1\varepsilon}:=1+e^{-1/\varepsilon}=:r_{2\varepsilon}
, then

[K_{\varepsilon}]\subseteq\langle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{r_{1\varepsilon}}(c_{1\varepsilon})\cup B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{r_{2\varepsilon}}(c_{2\varepsilon})\rangle=\langle(-2-e^{-1/\varepsilon},2+e^{-1/\varepsilon})_{\mathbb{R}}\rangle

but
s_{\varepsilon}=\text{Lebnum}((U_{j\varepsilon})_{j\in J},K_{\varepsilon})\leq e^{-1/\varepsilon}

because for
x=0
the largest ball contained in a set of the covering
is
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{e^{-1/\varepsilon}}(0)
.

8.2.
The dynamic compatibility condition

Definition 81
.

(i)

Let
[J]:=\{(j_{\varepsilon})\mid j_{\varepsilon}\in J,\forall\varepsilon\}=J^{I}
.

(ii)

Let
X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
Y\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
f\in\Set(X,Y)
.
Let
K\Subset_{\text{f}}X\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}
, and assume that for
all
j\in J
we have
f_{j}:=f|_{U_{j}\cap X}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U_{j}\cap X,Y)
.
Then we say that
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J}

satisfies the
dynamic compatibility condition (DCC) on the cover
\left(K\cap U_{j}\right)_{j\in J}

if for all
j\in J
there exist nets
\left(f_{j\varepsilon}\right)
defining

f_{j}
, for each
j\in J
, such that setting
U_{\bar{\jmath}}:=\langle U_{j_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}\rangle
,
we have:

(a)

\forall\bar{\jmath}=(j_{\varepsilon})\in[J]\,\forall[x_{\varepsilon}]\in U_{\bar{\jmath}}\cap K\,\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}:\ \left(\partial^{\alpha}f_{j_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\right)\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}^{d}
.

(b)

\forall\bar{\jmath}=(j_{\varepsilon}),\bar{h}=(h_{\varepsilon})\in[J]\,\forall[x_{\varepsilon}]\in K\cap U_{\bar{\jmath}}\cap U_{\bar{h}}:\ \left[f_{j_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\right]=\left[f_{h_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\right]
.

Finally, we say that
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J}

satisfies
the DCC on the cover
\left(U_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
if it satisfies
the DCC on each functionally compact set contained in
X
. The adjective

dynamic
underscores that we are considering
\varepsilon
-depending
indices
\bar{\jmath}=(j_{\varepsilon})\in[J]
.

Remark 82
.

(i)

Taking constant
\bar{\jmath}
and
\bar{h}
in Def.
81
.
(ii)(b)
,
we have that DCC is stronger than the classical compatibility condition
for
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
on
K\Subset_{\text{f}}X
.

(ii)

DCC is a necessary condition if the sections
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J}

glue into a GSF
f=[f_{\varepsilon}(-)]
because in this case we can take

f_{j\varepsilon}=f_{\varepsilon}
for all
j\in J
.

(iii)

The notation
U_{\bar{\jmath}}:=\langle U_{j_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}\rangle
used to
state the DCC was introduced merely for simplicity of notations. In
fact, in general it is not possible to prove the independence from
the representative net
(U_{j\varepsilon})
: if
U_{j}=\langle V_{j\varepsilon}\rangle
,
we can have
d_{\text{H}}(U_{j\varepsilon}^{c},V_{j\varepsilon}^{c})=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{j/\varepsilon}
,
but taking
\bar{\jmath}=(\varepsilon)
we would have
d_{\text{H}}(U_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon}^{c},V_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon}^{c})=\rho_{\varepsilon}

and hence
U_{\bar{\jmath}}=\langle U_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon}\rangle\neq V_{\bar{\jmath}}=\langle V_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon}\rangle
.

In the next and final subsection we prove that the DCC implies the
sheaf property.

8.3.
Proof of the sheaf property

Lemma 83
.

Let
K
,
K^{+}
be functionally compact sets
with
K\subseteq\text{int}(K^{+})\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Let
Y\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

and
f\in\Set(K^{+},Y)
. Let
K^{+}\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}
,
where
U_{j}=\langle U_{j\varepsilon}\rangle
are strongly internal sets and for
all
j\in J
we have
f_{j}:=f|_{U_{j}\cap K^{+}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U_{j}\cap K^{+},Y)
.
Let
K^{+}=[K_{\varepsilon}^{+}]
. Assume that, for some representatives

(f_{j\varepsilon})_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in J\\
\varepsilon\in I\end{subarray}}
of
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
we have

\forall m\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall j,k\in J:\ \sup_{K_{\varepsilon}^{+}\cap U_{j\varepsilon}\cap U_{k\varepsilon}}|f_{k\varepsilon}-f_{j\varepsilon}|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}.

(8.6)

Then
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,Y)
.

Proof.

Let
K=[K_{\varepsilon}]
. By changing the representative
(K_{\varepsilon}^{+})_{\varepsilon}

of
K^{+}
, we may assume that there exists
S\in\mathbb{N}
such that
B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{S}}(K_{\varepsilon})\subseteq K_{\varepsilon}^{+}
,

\forall\varepsilon

[
115
, Lemma 3.12]
. We have that
K_{\varepsilon}^{+}\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j,\varepsilon}
,

\forall\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, for some
\varepsilon_{0}>0
(proof by contradiction).
Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
. By compactness of
K_{\varepsilon}^{+}
,
K_{\varepsilon}^{+}\subseteq U_{j_{1},\varepsilon}\cup\dots\cup U_{j_{l_{\varepsilon}},\varepsilon}

for some
l_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
. Call
V_{k}:=U_{j_{k},\varepsilon}\setminus(U_{j_{1},\varepsilon}\cup\dots\cup U_{j_{k-1},\varepsilon})
,
and let

f_{\varepsilon}:=\sum_{k=1}^{l_{\varepsilon}}f_{j_{k},\varepsilon}1_{V_{k}}.

Then
f_{\varepsilon}
is locally integrable. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. By (
8.6
),
we find
\varepsilon_{m}>0
such that

\forall j,k\in J\,\forall\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{m}:\ \sup_{K_{\varepsilon}^{+}\cap U_{j,\varepsilon}\cap U_{k,\varepsilon}}|f_{k,\varepsilon}-f_{j,\varepsilon}|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}.

Further, by the definition of
f_{\varepsilon}
, we then also have

\forall j\in J\,\forall\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{m}:\ \sup_{K_{\varepsilon}^{+}\cap U_{j,\varepsilon}}|f_{\varepsilon}-f_{j,\varepsilon}|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}.

W.l.o.g.,
\varepsilon_{m}\downarrow 0
. Let
q_{\varepsilon}:=q
, for each
\varepsilon\in(\varepsilon_{(q+1)^{2}},\varepsilon_{q^{2}}]

(
q\in\mathbb{N}
). Then

\forall j\in J\,\forall\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}:\ \sup_{K_{\varepsilon}^{+}\cap U_{j,\varepsilon}}|f_{\varepsilon}-f_{j,\varepsilon}|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q_{\varepsilon}^{2}}.

Let
b
be a smooth map
\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
with
\int b=1
and
\mbox{stsupp}(b)\subseteq B_{1}(0)
,
and let

\delta_{q,\varepsilon}(x):=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-nq}b\left(\frac{x}{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}}\right).

We show that
(f_{\varepsilon}*\delta_{q_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}
is a representative
of
f
, thereby proving that
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
. We therefore
take
x=[x_{\varepsilon}]\in K
, and we prove that

(i)

(\partial^{\alpha}(f_{\varepsilon}*\delta_{q_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon})(x_{\varepsilon}))\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}^{d}
,

\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}

(ii)

[(f_{\varepsilon}*\delta_{q_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon})(x_{\varepsilon})]=f(x)
.

Let
j\in J
such that
x\in U_{j}
. Then there exists
S\in\mathbb{N}

such that
B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{S}}(x_{\varepsilon})\subseteq K_{\varepsilon}^{+}\cap U_{j,\varepsilon}
,

\forall^{0}\varepsilon
. To prove
(i)
and
(ii)
,
it suffices to see that
(\partial^{\alpha}(f_{\varepsilon}*\delta_{q_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon})(x_{\varepsilon})-\partial^{\alpha}f_{j,\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}))_{\varepsilon}

is negligible for each
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
. Then

\displaystyle\left|\partial^{\alpha}(f_{\varepsilon}*\delta_{q_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon})(x_{\varepsilon})-\partial^{\alpha}(f_{j,\varepsilon}*\delta_{q_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon})(x_{\varepsilon})\right|=\left|((f_{\varepsilon}-f_{j,\varepsilon})*\partial^{\alpha}\delta_{q_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon})(x_{\varepsilon})\right|

\displaystyle=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-|\alpha|q_{\varepsilon}}\left|\int(f_{\varepsilon}-f_{j,\varepsilon})(x_{\varepsilon}-\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q_{\varepsilon}}u)\partial^{\alpha}b(u)\,du\right|\leq C_{\alpha}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-|\alpha|q_{\varepsilon}}\sup_{B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q_{\varepsilon}}}(x_{\varepsilon})}|f_{\varepsilon}-f_{j,\varepsilon}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{(q_{\varepsilon}-|\alpha|)q_{\varepsilon}}

and

\displaystyle\left|\partial^{\alpha}(f_{j,\varepsilon}*\delta_{q_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon})(x_{\varepsilon})-\partial^{\alpha}f_{j,\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\right|=\left|\int(\partial^{\alpha}f_{j,\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}-y)-\partial^{\alpha}f_{j,\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}))\delta_{q_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}(y)\,dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{u\in B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q_{\varepsilon}}}(x_{\varepsilon})}|\partial^{\alpha}f_{j,\varepsilon}(u)-\partial^{\alpha}f_{j,\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})|\leq C\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q_{\varepsilon}}\max_{|\beta|=|\alpha|+1}\sup_{u\in B_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q_{\varepsilon}}}(x_{\varepsilon})}|\partial^{\beta}f_{j,\varepsilon}(u)|

\displaystyle\leq C\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q_{\varepsilon}-N_{j,\alpha}}

for sufficiently small
\varepsilon
, by Thm.
17

(i)
.
Combining both inequalities, we conclude that
(\partial^{\alpha}(f_{\varepsilon}*\delta_{q_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon})(x_{\varepsilon})-\partial^{\alpha}f_{j,\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}))_{\varepsilon}

is
\rho
-negligible.
∎

Lemma 84
.

Let
K
,
K^{+}
be functionally compact
sets with
K\subseteq\text{int}(K^{+})\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Let
Y\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

and
f\in\Set(K^{+},Y)
. Let
K^{+}\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}
,
where for all
j\in J
we have
f_{j}:=f|_{U_{j}\cap K^{+}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U_{j}\cap K^{+},Y)

and
U_{j}
is a strongly internal set. Assume that
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J}

satisfies the DCC on
the cover
\left(K^{+}\cap U_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
.

Then
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,Y)
.

Proof.

Let
s_{\varepsilon}:=\frac{1}{2}\text{Lebnum}\left(\left(U_{j\varepsilon}\right)_{j\in J},K_{\varepsilon}^{+}\right)
.
By Lemma
80
,
s:=[s_{\varepsilon}]>0
.

We define a cover
(V_{x})_{x\in K^{+}}
of
K^{+}
as follows.
Let
x=[x_{\varepsilon}]\in K^{+}
, where
x_{\varepsilon}\in K_{\varepsilon}^{+}
,
\forall\varepsilon
.
Then there exist
j_{\varepsilon}\in J
such that
B_{2s_{\varepsilon}}^{E}(x_{\varepsilon})\subseteq U_{j_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}
,

\forall\varepsilon
. We then define
V_{x}:=\langle B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{s_{\varepsilon}}(x_{\varepsilon})\rangle
,
and we denote
\bar{\jmath}(x):=[j_{\varepsilon}]\in[J]
. By condition
(ii)(a)
,

(f_{j_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon})
defines a generalized smooth map
f_{\bar{\jmath}(x)}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(V_{x}\cap K^{+},Y)
.

In order to conclude that
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(K,Y)
by Lemma
83
,
it suffices to show that:

(i)

f_{\bar{\jmath}(x)}=f|_{V_{x}\cap K^{+}}
,
\forall x\in K^{+}
.

(ii)

\forall m\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall x,y\in K^{+}:\ \sup_{K_{\varepsilon}^{+}\cap V_{x,\varepsilon}\cap V_{y,\varepsilon}}|f_{\bar{\jmath}(y),\varepsilon}-f_{\bar{\jmath}(x),\varepsilon}|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}
.

Proof of
(i)
: Let
x\in K^{+}
. Let
y\in V_{x}\cap K^{+}
.
We want to show that
f_{\bar{\jmath}(x)}(y)=f(y)
. As
y\in K^{+}
,
we have
y\in U_{j}
for some
j\in J
. Thus
y\in K^{+}\cap U_{j}\cap V_{x}\subseteq K^{+}\cap U_{j}\cap U_{\bar{\jmath}(x)}
,
and condition
(ii)(b)
yields
f(y)=f_{j}(y)=f_{\bar{\jmath}(x)}(y)
.

Proof of
(ii)
: By contradiction, suppose that there exists

m\in\mathbb{N}
and, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exist
\varepsilon_{n}>0
with

(\varepsilon_{n})_{n}
decreasingly tending to
0
and
x_{n},y_{n}\in K^{+}

and
z_{\varepsilon_{n}}\in K_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{+}\cap V_{x_{n},\varepsilon_{n}}\cap V_{y_{n},\varepsilon_{n}}

such that

|f_{j_{\varepsilon_{n}},\varepsilon_{n}}(z_{\varepsilon_{n}})-f_{h_{\varepsilon_{n}},\varepsilon_{n}}(z_{\varepsilon_{n}})|>\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{m}

(8.7)

where we denote
j_{\varepsilon_{n}}:=\bar{\jmath}(x_{n})_{\varepsilon_{n}}
and

h_{\varepsilon_{n}}:=\bar{\jmath}(y_{n})_{\varepsilon_{n}}\in J
. Then there exist

x_{\varepsilon_{n}},y_{\varepsilon_{n}}\in K_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{+}
such that
V_{x_{n},\varepsilon_{n}}=B_{s_{\varepsilon_{n}}}^{E}(x_{\varepsilon_{n}})

and
V_{y_{n},\varepsilon_{n}}=B_{s_{\varepsilon_{n}}}^{E}(y_{\varepsilon_{n}})
. For

\varepsilon\notin\{\varepsilon_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}
, let
z_{\varepsilon}\in K_{\varepsilon}^{+}

be arbitrary. Then
z\in K^{+}
. Let
j_{\varepsilon}:=h_{\varepsilon}:=\bar{\jmath}(z)_{\varepsilon}
,
for
\varepsilon\notin\{\varepsilon_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}
. Let
(z^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})
be any representative
of
z
. Then

\begin{cases}z^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\in B_{s_{\varepsilon}}^{E}(z_{\varepsilon})\subseteq U_{j_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}=U_{h_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}&\forall^{0}\varepsilon\notin\{\varepsilon_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}\\
z^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\in B_{s_{\varepsilon_{n}}}^{E}(z_{\varepsilon_{n}})\subseteq B_{2s_{\varepsilon_{n}}}^{E}(x_{\varepsilon_{n}})\subseteq U_{j_{\varepsilon_{n}},\varepsilon_{n}}&\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\text{ large enough}\end{cases}

and similarly
z^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\in U_{h_{\varepsilon_{n}},\varepsilon_{n}}
for large
enough
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus
z\in K^{+}\cap U_{\bar{\jmath}}\cap U_{\bar{h}}
,
and condition
(ii)(b)
contradicts (
8.7
).
∎

Using Thm.
77
and a functionally
compact exhaustion, see Def.
78
, we get

Theorem 85
.

Let
X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be a set
that admits a functionally compact exhaustion,
Y\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

and
f\in\Set(X,Y)
. Let
X\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}
, where
for all
j\in J
we have
f_{j}:=f|_{U_{j}\cap X}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U_{j}\cap X,Y)

and
U_{j}
is a strongly internal set. Assume that
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J}

satisfies the DCC on
the cover
\left(U_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
.

Then
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,Y)
.

The usual sheaf properties both for Schwartz distributions and for
Colombeau generalized functions do not need any stronger compatibility
condition, which ultimately stems from the possibility to use, for
these generalized functions, only (near-)standard points (recall Thm.
20

and Thm.
21
). This is proved in the following
result, which generalizes the aforementioned sheaf properties (see
e.g.
[
49
]
).

Theorem 86
.

Let
X\subseteq{\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\right)}^{\bullet}
,

Y\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and let
f:X\longrightarrow Y
be a set-theoretical map.
Suppose that
X\subseteq\bigcup_{x\in X}B_{r_{x}}(x)
, where
r_{x}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}

for all
x
, and that

f|_{B_{r_{x}}(x)\cap X}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(B_{r_{x}}(x)\cap X,Y)

for all
x\in X
. Then
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,Y)
.

Proof.

For every
x\in X
, let
f|_{B_{r_{x}}(x)}=:v^{x}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(B_{r_{x}}(x)\cap X)

and let
v^{x}
be defined by the net
(v_{\varepsilon}^{x})
with
v_{\varepsilon}^{x}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Recall that by
x^{\circ}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
we denote the standard part of
any
x\in(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n})^{\bullet}
. Pick a countable, locally finite
open (in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
) refinement
(U_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of
(B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}{}_{r_{x}/2}(x^{\circ}))_{x\in X}

and let
(\chi_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a partition of unity with
\mbox{stsupp}\chi_{i}\Subset U_{i}

for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
. For any
i\in\mathbb{N}
pick
x_{i}\in X
such that

U_{i}\subseteq B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{r_{x_{i}}/2}(x_{i}^{\circ})
and set

f_{\varepsilon}:=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\chi_{i}v_{\varepsilon}^{x_{i}}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Then the net
(f_{\varepsilon})
defines a GSF of the type
X\longrightarrow Y
: indeed,
we will show that
f(z)=v^{z}(z)=[f_{\varepsilon}(z_{\varepsilon})]
for all
z=[z_{\varepsilon}]\in X
:

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}(z_{\varepsilon})-v_{\varepsilon}^{z}(z_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\chi_{i}(z_{\varepsilon})(v_{\varepsilon}^{x_{i}}(z_{\varepsilon})-v_{\varepsilon}^{z}(z_{\varepsilon}))=

\displaystyle=\sum_{\{i\mid z^{\circ}\in B_{3r_{x_{i}}/4}(x_{i})\}}\chi_{i}(z_{\varepsilon})(v_{\varepsilon}^{x_{i}}(z_{\varepsilon})-v_{\varepsilon}^{z}(z_{\varepsilon}))\ +

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\sum_{\{i\mid z^{\circ}\not\in B_{3r_{x_{i}}/4}(x_{i})\}}\chi_{i}(z_{\varepsilon})(v_{\varepsilon}^{x_{i}}(z_{\varepsilon})-v_{\varepsilon}^{z}(z_{\varepsilon}))=

\displaystyle=:A_{\varepsilon}+B_{\varepsilon}.

Since
z_{\varepsilon}\to z^{\circ}
, for small
\varepsilon
all
z_{\varepsilon}

remain in a compact set and since the supports of the
\chi_{i}

form a locally finite family it follows that both
A_{\varepsilon}
and

B_{\varepsilon}
are in fact finite sums for small
\varepsilon
. To estimate
the summands in
A_{\varepsilon}
, note that
z^{\circ}\in B_{3r_{x_{i}}/4}(x_{i})

implies that
z\in B_{r_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})
, so
v^{x_{i}}(z)=f|_{B_{r_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}(z)=f(z)=v^{z}(z)
.
Hence
\left[A_{\varepsilon}\right]=0
. Concerning
B_{\varepsilon}
,
z^{\circ}\not\in B_{3r_{x_{i}}/4}(x_{i})

implies that
|z^{\circ}-x_{i}^{\circ}|>r_{x_{i}}/2
. On the other
hand, if
\chi_{i}(z_{\varepsilon})\not=0
then
z_{\varepsilon}\in U_{i}\subseteq B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{r_{x_{i}}/2}(x_{i}^{\circ})
,
implying
|z^{\circ}-x_{i}^{\circ}|\leq r_{x_{i}}/2
. Hence
B_{\varepsilon}=0
.
Consequently,
\left[f_{\varepsilon}(z_{\varepsilon})\right]=\left[v_{\varepsilon}^{z}(z_{\varepsilon})\right]
,
as claimed.
∎

The following is the sheaf property for Fermat covers.

Corollary 87
.

Let
X\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
Y\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

and let
f:X\longrightarrow Y
be a set-theoretical map. Suppose that
X\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}
,
where each
U_{j}
is a large open set, and that

f|_{U_{j}\cap X}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U_{j}\cap X,Y)

for all
j\in J
. Then

(i)

If
X\subseteq{\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\right)}^{\bullet}
,
then
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,Y)
.

(ii)

If
X
contains its subpoints and all points
of
X
are finite, then
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(X,Y)
.

Proof.

To prove property
(i)
, let
s={x^{\circ}}
,
x\in X
.
Then
x\in U_{j_{x}}
for some
j_{x}\in J
, and hence
B_{r_{j_{x}}}(x)\subseteq U_{j_{x}}

for some
r_{j_{x}}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
. Therefore
s={x^{\circ}}\in B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{r_{j_{x}}}({x^{\circ}})

and so
{X^{\circ}}\subseteq\bigcup_{x\in X}B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}_{r_{j_{x}}}({x^{\circ}})
.
Claim
(i)
now follows directly from Cor.
86
.

Property
(ii)
follows by
(i)

and Thm.
21
applied to
f|_{X^{\prime}}
, where

X^{\prime}:=\{x\in X\mid x\text{ is near-standard}\}
.
∎

It is now natural to ask whether the sheaf property Thm.
85

could be inscribed into the general notion of sheaf on a site. This
is one of the aims of the next Sec.
9
.

9.
The Grothendieck topos of generalized
smooth functions

As we argued in the introduction, function spaces and Cartesian closedness
are considered by many authors as important features for mathematics
and mathematical physics. Even if Colombeau’s theory of generalized
functions can be extended to any locally convex space
E
, on the
other hand, in
[
71
]
(p. 2) it is stated that:
“locally
convex topology is not appropriate for non-linear questions in infinite
dimensions
”, and indeed

a different approach to infinite
dimensional spaces is to embed smooth manifolds into a Cartesian closed
category
\mathcal{C}
(see
[
36
]
for a review of this type
of approaches). Similar lines of thought can be found in
[
62
,
63
]
,
but where generalized functions are seen as functionals, hence not
following Cauchy-Dirac’s original conception but Schwartz’ conception
instead. We first motivate and introduce the few notions of category
theory that we need in the present section. Indeed, only basic preliminaries
of category theory are needed to understand this section: definition
of category and basic examples, functors and natural transformation.
Our basic references for this section are
[
77
,
58
,
7
]
.
As it is customary, we write
D\in\mathbb{D}
to denote that
D

is an object of the category
\mathbb{D}
, we write
A\xrightarrow{\ \ f\ \ }B

in

\mathbb{D}
to say that
f\in\mathbb{D}(A,B)
and
\mathbb{D}^{\text{op}}

for the opposite of
\mathbb{D}
(see e.g.
[
78
]
). Only in
this section, we use both the notations
f\cdot g:=g\circ f
for
arrows
X\xrightarrow{\ \ f\ \ }Y\xrightarrow{\ \ g\ \ }Z
in some category, and the notation
\bar{\jmath}=\left(\bar{\jmath}_{\varepsilon}\right)\in[J]
.

9.1.
Coverages, sheaves and sites

The notion of coverage on a category allows one to define more abstractly
the concept of sheaf without being forced to consider a topological
space. Nevertheless, the classical example to keep in mind to have
a first understanding of the following definitions is a sheaf (e.g. of
continuous functions) defined on the poset of open sets
\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{D}(X)

in some topological space
X
.

We first define families with common codomain
D
:

Definition 88
.

Let
\mathbb{D}
be a category and let
D\in\mathbb{D}
.
Then we say that
\mathcal{F}\in\text{Fam}(D)
is a
family
with common codomain
D
if there exist a set
J\in\Set
and families

\left(D_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
,
\left(i_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
such
that:

(i)

D_{j}\xrightarrow{\ \ i_{j}\ \ }D
in
\mathbb{D}

for all
j\in J
.

(ii)

\mathcal{F}=\left(D_{j}\xrightarrow{\ \ i_{j}\ \ }D\right)_{j\in J}
.

A coverage is a class of families with a common codomain
that is closed with respect to pullback, in the precise sense stated
in the following

Definition 89
.

Let
\mathbb{D}
be a category, then we say
that
\Gamma
is a
coverage on
\mathbb{D}
if:

(i)

\Gamma:\text{Obj}(\mathbb{D})\longrightarrow\Set
,
where
\text{Obj}(\mathbb{D})
is the class of objects of the category

\mathbb{D}
.

(ii)

\forall D\in\mathbb{D}:\ \Gamma(D)\subseteq\text{Fam}(D)
. Families
in
\Gamma(D)
are called
covering families of
D
.

(iii)

If
D\in\mathbb{D}
,
\left(D_{j}\xrightarrow{\ \ i_{j}\ \ }D\right)_{j\in J}\in\Gamma(D)

is a covering family of
D
, and
C\xrightarrow{\ \ g\ \ }D
is an arbitrary arrow
of
\mathbb{D}
, then there exists a covering family of
C
,
\left(C_{k}\xrightarrow{\ \ h_{k}\ \ }C\right)_{k\in K}\in\Gamma(C)

such that

\forall k\in K\,\exists j\in J\,\exists\bar{g}:\begin{array}[]{cc}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 12.39133pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.39133pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-2.66666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.80824pt\raise 5.9611pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{h_{k}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.73856pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-17.25346pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bar{g}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.65973pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 36.73856pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-3.41666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.6698pt\raise-17.25346pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.6698pt\raise-25.09027pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-12.38525pt\raise-34.50693pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-1.98611pt\hbox{$\textstyle{D_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.05234pt\raise-28.20485pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.32986pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i_{j}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.39133pt\raise-34.50693pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 36.39133pt\raise-34.50693pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-3.41666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{D}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}

(9.1)

(iv)

A pair
(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
, of a category and a coverage on it,
is called a
site
.

For example, let
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{OGC}^{\infty}
be the category of sharply open
sets
U\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{u}
(all possible dimensions
u\in\mathbb{N}
are
included) and GSF. Let
\Gamma(U)
contains open coverings and inclusions:

\left(U_{j}\xrightarrow{\ \ i_{j}\ \ }U\right)_{j\in J}\in\Gamma(U)
if and only
if
U_{j}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{OGC}^{\infty}
,
i_{j}:U_{j}\hookrightarrow U
and
\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}=U
.
Then
(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{OGC}^{\infty},\Gamma)
is a site and property (
9.1
)
holds simply by taking
K=J
and
C_{j}:=g^{-1}(U_{j})\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{OGC}^{\infty}

as covering family of
C
, and
\bar{g}:=g|_{C_{j}}
. Note that
these simple steps do not work in the category
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{SGC}^{\infty}
of strongly
internal sets and GSF because in general
g^{-1}(U_{j})
is not strongly
internal (only the inclusion
g^{-1}\left(\langle A_{\varepsilon}\rangle\right)\subseteq\langle g_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(A_{\varepsilon})\rangle

holds). In this case, a general method is to express the open set

g^{-1}(U_{j})
as a union of strongly internal sets. This implies
that we have to take a different index set
K
for the covering family

C_{k}\hookrightarrow C
.

Using the notion of coverage, we can define the notion of compatible
family:

Definition 90
.

Let
(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
be a site and
F:\mathbb{D}^{\text{op}}\longrightarrow\Set

be a presheaf. Let
\mathcal{F}=\left(D_{j}\xrightarrow{\ \ i_{j}\ \ }D\right)_{j\in J}\in\Gamma(D)

be a covering family of
D\in\mathbb{D}
. Then, we say that
(f_{j})_{j\in J}

are compatible on
\mathcal{F}
(rel.
F
)
if the following
conditions hold:

(i)

f_{j}\in F(D_{j})
for all
j\in J
. In
this case
f_{j}
is called a
section
.

(ii)

For all
g
,
c
and
j
,
h\in J
, we have

\begin{array}[]{cc}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 12.38525pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.93124pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-3.41666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.84042pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{c}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.38525pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-17.25346pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.65973pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 36.38525pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-2.66666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{D_{h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.2546pt\raise-17.25346pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.78542pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i_{h}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.2546pt\raise-25.09027pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-12.38525pt\raise-34.50693pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-1.98611pt\hbox{$\textstyle{D_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.34473pt\raise-28.20485pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.32986pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i_{j}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.97612pt\raise-34.50693pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.97612pt\raise-34.50693pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\raise-3.41666pt\hbox{$\textstyle{D}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}\quad\Rightarrow\quad F(g)(f_{j})=F(c)(f_{h}).

(9.2)

A typical way to apply (
9.2
) is to construct
a sort of intersection object
C=D_{h}\cap D_{j}
and to take as

c
,
g
the inclusions. Then, if
F=\mathbb{D}(-,Y)
, the equality
in (
9.2
) reduces to the usual compatibility condition

f_{j}|_{D_{h}\cap D_{j}}=f_{h}|_{D_{h}\cap D_{j}}
.

We can finally define the notion of sheaf on a site:

Definition 91
.

Let
(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
be a site, then we say
that
F
is a
sheaf on
(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
, and we write

F\in\text{Sh}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
if

(i)

F:\mathbb{D}^{\text{op}}\longrightarrow\Set
(i.e.
F
is a presheaf).

(ii)

If
\mathcal{F}=\left(D_{j}\xrightarrow{\ \ i_{j}\ \ }D\right)_{j\in J}\in\Gamma(D)

is a covering family of
D\in\mathbb{D}
and
(f_{j})_{j\in J}

are compatible on
\mathcal{F}
(rel.
F
), then

\exists!f\in F(D)\,\forall j\in J:\ F(i_{j})(f)=f_{j}.

(9.3)

In the classical example of continuous functions on a topological
space,
F=\mathcal{C}^{0}(-,Y)
and the equality in (
9.3
)
becomes
f|_{D_{j}}=f_{j}
. Note that, even if the category of open
sets and GSF
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{OGC}^{\infty}
is a site, example
76
shows
that
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{O}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{OGC}^{\infty}(-,Y)
is not a sheaf.

9.2.
The category of glueable functions

As we mentioned in the introduction to Sec.
8
,
our main aim here is to show that the DCC is strictly related to the
aforementioned definition of sheaf on a site. The strategy we will
follow is:

(a)

Define a category
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}\supseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{SGC}^{\infty}
.

(b)

Define a coverage on
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
.

(c)

Show that
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(-,\mathcal{Y})
is a
sheaf using Thm.
85
and hence the DCC.

Intuitively, we already think that a family of strongly
internal sets
\left(U_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
is a coverage of the
strongly internal set
U
if, simply,
U\subseteq\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}
;
we also intuitively think that
(f_{j})_{j\in J}
are compatible
sections if DCC holds. The following definition reflects this intuition:

Definition 92
.

Let
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
be the category of
glueable
families
, whose objects are non empty families
\left(U_{j}\right)_{j\in J}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}

of strongly internal sets in some space
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{u}
:

J\neq\emptyset,\ \exists u\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall j\in J:\ \mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{u}\supseteq U_{j}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{SGC}^{\infty}.

We say that

\mathcal{X}\xrightarrow{\ \ \varphi\ \ }\mathcal{Y}\quad\text{in}\quad\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}

if
\mathcal{X}=\left(U_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
,
\mathcal{Y}=\left(V_{h}\right)_{h\in H}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}

and
\varphi=\left(\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J},\alpha\right)
, where:

(i)

The map
\alpha\in\Set(J,H)
is called a
reparametrization
.

(ii)

The family of GSF
f_{j}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{SGC}^{\infty}(U_{j},V_{\alpha(j)})
,

j\in J
, satisfies the DCC on
U:=\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}
.

To state condition
(ii)
more explicitly, let

u
,
v\in\mathbb{N}
be the dimensions of
\left(U_{j}\right)_{j\in J}

and
\left(V_{h}\right)_{h\in H}
resp. (i.e.
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{u}\supseteq U_{j}

and
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{v}\supseteq V_{h}
for all
j
,
h
), and set
V:=\bigcup_{h\in H}V_{h}
.
Then
(ii)
asks that there exists
\left(U_{j\varepsilon}\right)_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in J\\
\varepsilon\in I\end{subarray}}
such that for all
K\Subset_{\text{f}}U
there exists
(f_{j\varepsilon})_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in J\\
\varepsilon\in I\end{subarray}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{u},\mathbb{R}^{v})
such that:

(ii.a)

f_{j}=\left[f_{j\varepsilon}(-)\right]|_{U_{j}}
for
each
j\in J
.

(ii.b)

\left[f_{\bar{\jmath}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}(-)\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U_{\bar{\jmath}}\cap K,V_{\bar{\jmath}\cdot\alpha}\cap V)

for all
\bar{\jmath}=\left(\bar{\jmath}_{\varepsilon}\right)\in[J]
, where

U_{\bar{\jmath}}:=\langle U_{\bar{\jmath}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}\rangle
. Note that

I\xrightarrow{\ \ \bar{\jmath}\ \ }J\xrightarrow{\ \ \alpha\ \ }H
and hence
\bar{\jmath}\cdot\alpha=\alpha\circ\bar{\jmath}\in[H]
.

(ii.c)

\left[f_{\bar{\jmath}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}(-)\right]=\left[f_{\bar{h}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}(-)\right]

on
U_{\bar{\jmath}}\cap U_{\bar{h}}\cap K
for all
\bar{\jmath}
,

\bar{h}\in[J]
.

Composition and identities in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
are defined as follows:
Let

\mathcal{X}\xrightarrow{\ \ \varphi\ \ }\mathcal{Y}\xrightarrow{\ \ \psi\ \ }\mathcal{Z}\quad\text{in}\quad\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}

(9.4)

and set
\mathcal{X}=\left(U_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
,
\mathcal{Y}=\left(V_{h}\right)_{h\in H}
,

\mathcal{Z}=\left(W_{l}\right)_{l\in L}
,
\varphi=\left(\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J},\alpha\right)
,

\psi=\left(\left(g_{h}\right)_{h\in H},\beta\right)
. Then

\displaystyle U_{j}\xrightarrow{\ \ f_{j}\ \ }V_{\alpha(j)}\xrightarrow{\ \ g_{\alpha(j)}\ \ }W_{\beta(\alpha(j))}\quad\forall j\in J

\displaystyle J\xrightarrow{\ \ \alpha\ \ }H\xrightarrow{\ \ \beta\ \ }L,

and we hence set

\displaystyle\varphi\cdot\psi

\displaystyle:=\left(\left(f_{j}\cdot g_{\alpha(j)}\right)_{j\in J},\alpha\cdot\beta\right)

\displaystyle 1_{\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle:=\left(\left(1_{U_{j}}\right)_{j\in J},1_{J}\right).

The following lemma confirms the correctness of this definition.

Lemma 93
.

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
is a category.

Proof.

We essentially have to prove the closure with respect to composition,
i.e. that (
9.4
) implies
\varphi\cdot\psi:\mathcal{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Z}

in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
. We implicitly use the notations of the previous definition.
For all
K=[K_{\varepsilon}]\Subset_{\text{f}}U
, we have
f_{\bar{\jmath}}:=\left[f_{\bar{\jmath}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}(-)\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U_{\bar{\jmath}}\cap K,V_{\bar{\jmath}\cdot\alpha}\cap V)
,
and hence for
\bar{\jmath}\in[J]
we get
f_{\bar{\jmath}}\left(\left[U_{\bar{\jmath}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}\cap K_{\varepsilon}\right]\right)=:\hat{K}\Subset_{\text{f}}V_{\bar{\jmath}\cdot\alpha}\cap V
.
Using
\hat{K}
and
\bar{\jmath}\cdot\alpha=:\bar{h}\in[H]
with
the arrow
\psi
, we obtain
g_{\bar{h}}:=\left[g_{\bar{h}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}(-)\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(V_{\bar{h}}\cap\hat{K},W_{\bar{h}\cdot\beta}\cap W)

for some nets
\left(g_{h\varepsilon}\right)_{h,\varepsilon}
. Therefore

\left(f_{\bar{\jmath}}\cdot g_{\bar{\jmath}\cdot\alpha}\right)|_{U_{\bar{\jmath}}\cap K}=\left[g_{\alpha(\bar{\jmath}_{\varepsilon}),\varepsilon}\left(f_{\bar{\jmath}_{\varepsilon},\varepsilon}(-)\right)\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U_{\bar{\jmath}}\cap K,W_{\bar{\jmath}\cdot\alpha\cdot\beta}\cap W).

This shows that condition
(ii.b)
of Def.
92

holds for
\varphi\cdot\psi=\left(\left(f_{j}\cdot g_{\alpha(j)}\right)_{j\in J},\alpha\cdot\beta\right)
.
To prove condition
(ii.c)
take
x=[x_{\varepsilon}]\in U_{\bar{\jmath}}\cap U_{\bar{l}}\cap K
,
then
f_{\bar{\jmath}}(x)=f_{\bar{l}}(x)\in V_{\bar{\jmath}\cdot\alpha}\cap V_{\bar{l}\cdot\alpha}\cap\hat{K}

and hence
g_{\bar{\jmath}\cdot\alpha}\left(f_{\bar{\jmath}}(x)\right)=g_{\bar{l}\cdot\alpha}\left(f_{\bar{l}}(x)\right)
,
which is our conclusion. Properties of identities trivially hold.
∎

Note that
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{SGC}^{\infty}\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
through the embedding:

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{SGC}^{\infty}\mapsto\left(U\right)_{\bar{\mathbb{1}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{SGC}^{\infty}(U,V)\mapsto\left(\left(f\right)_{\bar{\mathbb{1}}},\bar{\mathbb{1}}\longrightarrow\bar{\mathbb{1}}\right)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}\left(\left(U\right)_{\bar{\mathbb{1}}},\left(V\right)_{\bar{\mathbb{1}}}\right),

where
\bar{\mathbb{1}}:=\{*\}
is any singleton set. The converse
is also possible using the sheaf Thm.
85
:
In fact, if
