\left(\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J},\alpha\right)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}\left(\left(U_{j}\right)_{j\in J},\left(V_{h}\right)_{h\in H}\right)
,
then the DCC holds and hence there exists a unique
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{SGC}^{\infty}(U,V)

such that
f|_{U_{j}}=f_{j}
for all
j\in J
. If we set
\text{gl}\left(\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J},\alpha\right):=f

then

\displaystyle\text{gl}\left(\varphi\cdot\psi\right)

\displaystyle=\text{gl}\left(\left(f_{j}\cdot g_{\alpha(j)}\right)_{j\in J},\alpha\cdot\beta\right)=\text{gl}\left(\varphi\right)\cdot\text{gl}\left(\psi\right)

because setting
\text{gl}(\varphi)=:f
and
\text{gl}(\psi)=:g
, we
have
\left(f\cdot g\right)|_{U_{j}}=f|_{U_{j}}\cdot g|_{V_{\alpha(j)}}=f_{j}\cdot g_{\alpha(j)}
,
i.e. the unique GSF obtained by gluing
\left(f_{j}\cdot g_{\alpha(j)}\right)_{j\in J}

is
f\cdot g
. Finally
\text{gl}\left(1_{\mathcal{X}}\right)=1_{U}=1_{\text{gl}(\mathcal{X})}

and hence
\text{gl}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{S}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{SGC}^{\infty}
is a (clearly non injective)
functor.

9.3.
Coverage of glueable functions

We now introduce a coverage on the category
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
of glueable
families:

Definition 94
.

Let
\mathcal{E}=\left(W_{e}\right)_{e\in E}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
,
then we say that
\gamma\in\Gamma(\mathcal{E})
if there exists a
non empty
J\in\Set
such that:

(i)

\gamma=\left(\gamma_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
and

\gamma_{j}=\left(\left(i_{h}\right)_{h\in J},\delta\right)
for
all
j\in J
.

(ii)

J\xrightarrow{\ \ \delta\ \ }E
is a surjective map.

(iii)

\textstyle{\!\!\!\!i_{j}:D_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{\delta(j)}}
for all
j\in J
, where
\left(D_{j}\right)_{j\in J}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}

. Because of this property,
\delta
is called a
refinement
map
.

(iv)

W_{e}=\bigcup\left\{D_{j}\mid\delta(j)=e,\ j\in J\right\}

for all
e\in E
.

Remark 95
.

(i)

Note that the index set
J
of
\gamma=\left(\gamma_{j}\right)_{j\in J}

is the same used in
\left(i_{h}\right)_{h\in J}
. Moreover, the
two components of
\gamma_{j}
do not depend on
j\in J
. This may
appear to be a strange property for a coverage, but note that our
intuition here is guided by viewing the inclusions
\textstyle{(D_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{j}}
\textstyle{W)_{j\in J}}
as a coverage of
W:=\bigcup_{e\in E}W_{e}
. On the other hand, note
that in Def.
92
of glueable families, in the DCC

(ii)
we need the whole family
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J}

and not only the single GSF
f_{j}
. Similarly, we need to consider
the entire family of inclusions
\left(i_{h}\right)_{h\in J}
and
not the single
i_{h}
. For this reason, we cannot directly consider
the family
\textstyle{(D_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{j}}
\textstyle{W)_{j\in J},}
made of single inclusions, as a coverage.

(ii)

Condition
(iv)
implies
W=\bigcup_{j\in J}D_{j}=\bigcup_{j\in J}W_{\delta(j)}
.
We will see more clearly later that this condition allows us to prove
the uniqueness part of (
9.3
).

(iii)

If
W_{e}\neq\emptyset
for all
e\in E
, then
(iv)

directly implies that
\delta
has to be a surjective map.

Theorem 96
.

\Gamma
is a coverage on
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
.

Proof.

From Def.
94
.
(iii)
it follows that

\gamma_{j}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}\left(\left(D_{j}\right)_{j\in J},\mathcal{E}\right)
,
i.e. property Def.
88
.
(i)
.
To prove the closure with respect to pullbacks, take
\eta\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E})
,
where

Since

.

Set

,

\nu:(j,h,c)\in K\mapsto c\in C
,
\textstyle{a_{k}:B_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{\nu(k)},}
and

.
Then
K
is non empty because
C
,
H_{j}
,

, and
we have

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{k\in K}B_{k}=\bigcup_{c\in C}\bigcup_{j\in J}\bigcup_{h\in H_{j}}B_{jhc}=\bigcup_{c\in C}V_{c}\cap\bigcup_{j\in J}\bigcup_{h\in H_{j}}B_{jh}=V\cap\bigcup_{j\in J}g^{-1}(D_{j})=

To prove property
(iv)
of Def.
94

for the new covering family
\left(\alpha_{k}\right)_{k\in K}
, take

, so that

. Thereby,

for some

, and hence

for
some

. Setting

, we have
\nu(k)=c

and

. This shows that

.
Finally, let
\left(\delta\right)_{\text{l}}^{-1}
be any left inverse
of
\delta
, i.e.
\left(\delta\right)_{\text{l}}^{-1}\cdot\delta=\delta\circ\left(\delta\right)_{\text{l}}^{-1}=1_{E}

(recall Def.
94
.
(ii)
), and setting

\mathcal{B}_{k}:=\left(B_{k}\right)_{k\in K}
,
\mu:=\left(\left(g_{k}|_{B_{k}}\right)_{k\in K},\nu\cdot\beta\cdot\left(\delta\right)_{\text{l}}^{-1}\right)

we have

\forall k\in K\,\exists j\in J\,\exists\mu:\begin{array}[]{cc}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern i.e. the claim Def.

.

Definition 97
.

The category of sheaves
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\text{T}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}:=\text{Sh}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty},\Gamma\right)

(and natural transformations as arrows) is called the

(see e.g.

,

,
7
]

and references therein).

9.4.
The sheaf of glueable functions

We are now able to show that the DCC is the key property to prove
the following

Theorem 98
.

For each
\mathcal{Y}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
, the
functor
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(-,\mathcal{Y})
is a sheaf on the site
\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty},\Gamma\right)
,
i.e. it satisfies Def.
91
:
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(-,\mathcal{Y})\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\text{T}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty},\Gamma\right)
.

Proof.

We use the notations of Def.
94
. Let
\left(\gamma_{j}\right)_{j\in J}=\left(\left(i_{h}\right)_{h\in J},\delta\right)_{j\in J}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{E})

be a covering family and let
\varphi_{j}=\left(\left(f_{h}^{j}\right)_{h\in J},\alpha^{j}\right)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{D}_{j},\mathcal{Y})
,

j\in J
, be a compatible family of sections, where
\mathcal{D}_{j}:=\left(D_{h}\right)_{h\in J}=:\mathcal{D}_{0}

(recall Rem.
95
.
(i)
about the independence
from
j\in J
). Note explicitly that by Def.
94
.
(i)

the covering family
\left(\gamma_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
is indexed
by the same set
J
as its inclusions
\left(i_{h}\right)_{h\in J}
;
moreover, the glueable family
\left(f_{h}^{j}\right)_{h\in J}
is
also indexed by
J
because by Def.
92
, any arrow
in the category
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
is indexed by the same set of its domain
which, in this case, is
\mathcal{D}_{0}=\left(D_{h}\right)_{h\in J}
.
Set
\mathcal{Y}=:\left(V_{l}\right)_{l\in L}
. We first want to
prove that the compatibility of sections
\left(\varphi_{j}\right)_{j\in J}

allows us to show that both
\left(f_{h}^{j}\right)_{h\in J}
and

\alpha^{j}
do not actually depend on
j
. We therefore take
i\in J

and define
\mathcal{D}_{0}\cap\mathcal{D}_{0}:=\left(D_{h}\cap D_{k}\right)_{(h,k)\in J^{2}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
,

\textstyle{i_{hk}:D_{h}\cap D_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D_{h},}

\textstyle{i_{kh}:D_{h}\cap D_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D_{k},}
\nu_{1}:(h,k)\in J^{2}\mapsto h\in J
,
\nu_{2}:(h,k)\in J^{2}\mapsto k\in J
,

\iota_{1}:=\left(\left(i_{hk}\right)_{(h,k)\in J^{2}},\nu_{1}\right)
,

\iota_{2}:=\left(\left(i_{kh}\right)_{(h,k)\in J^{2}},\nu_{2}\right)
.
The compatibility condition (
9.2
) for the functor
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(-,\mathcal{Y})

yields

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(-,\mathcal{Y})(\iota_{1})(\varphi_{j})

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(-,\mathcal{Y})(\iota_{2})(\varphi_{i})

\displaystyle\left(\left(i_{hk}\cdot f_{\nu_{1}(h,k)}^{j}\right)_{(h,k)\in J^{2}},\nu_{1}\cdot\alpha^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(i_{kh}\cdot f_{\nu_{2}(h,k)}^{i}\right)_{(h,k)\in J^{2}},\nu_{2}\cdot\alpha^{i}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\left(f_{h}^{j}|_{D_{h}\cap D_{k}}\right)_{hk},\nu_{1}\cdot\alpha^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(f_{k}^{i}|_{D_{h}\cap D_{k}}\right)_{hk},\nu_{2}\cdot\alpha^{i}\right).

Thereby, for
h=k
we get
f_{h}^{j}=f_{h}^{i}
and, for arbitrary

h
,
k\in J
, we also have
(\nu_{1}\cdot\alpha^{j})(h,k)=(\nu_{2}\cdot\alpha^{i})(h,k)
,
i.e.
\alpha^{j}(h)=\alpha^{i}(k)
. This equality, since
J\neq\emptyset
,
implies that
\alpha^{i}=\alpha^{j}=:\alpha_{\text{c}}\in L
is constant.
Therefore, as a consequence of the compatibility condition, we have
that both components of
\varphi_{j}
do not depend on
j\in J
: our
sections can hence be simply written as
\varphi_{j}=:\left(\left(f_{h}\right)_{h\in J},\alpha_{\text{c}}\right)
.
Note also that all the GSF
f_{h}:D_{h}\longrightarrow V:=V_{\alpha_{\text{c}}}

have the same codomain. The glueable family
\left(f_{h}\right)_{h\in J}

satisfies the DCC because of Def.
92
.
(ii)

and we can hence apply Thm.
85
to obtain
a unique
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(D,V)
such that
f|_{D_{j}}=f_{j}
for each
j\in J
,
where
D=\bigcup_{j\in J}D_{j}
. We can finally set
\bar{\alpha}:e\in E\mapsto\alpha_{\text{c}}\in L

and
\varphi:=\left(\left(f|_{W_{e}}\right)_{e\in E},\bar{\alpha}\right)

to obtain the existence part of the conclusion:

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(-,\mathcal{Y})(\gamma_{j})(\varphi)=\gamma_{j}\cdot\varphi=\left(\left(f|_{W_{\delta(h)}\cap D_{h}}\right)_{h\in J},\delta\cdot\bar{\alpha}\right)=\left(\left(f|_{D_{h}}\right)_{h\in J},\alpha\right)=\varphi_{j}.

To prove the uniqueness of the glued section, assume that
\hat{\varphi}=\left(\left(\hat{f}_{e}\right)_{e\in E},\hat{\alpha}\right)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{Y})

is another section such that
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(-,\mathcal{Y})(\gamma_{j})(\hat{\varphi})=\varphi_{j}

for all
j\in J
. This equality gives

\left(\left(\hat{f}_{\delta(h)}|_{D_{h}}\right)_{h\in J},\delta\cdot\hat{\alpha}\right)=\left(\left(f_{h}\right)_{h\in J},\alpha\right)

(9.5)

Take
e\in E
and
x\in W_{e}
, then condition Def.
94
.
(iv)

yields the existence of
h\in J
such that
\delta(h)=e
and
x\in D_{h}
.
Therefore, using (
9.5
) we obtain

\hat{f}_{e}(x)=\hat{f}_{\delta(h)}|_{D_{h}\cap W_{e}}(x)=f_{h}|_{W_{e}}(x)=f|_{W_{e}}(x),

i.e.
\hat{f}_{e}=f|_{W_{e}}
for all
e\in E
. Finally, (
9.5
)
also gives
\hat{\alpha}=\left(\delta\right)_{\text{l}}^{-1}\cdot\alpha
.
∎

9.5.
Concrete sites and generalized diffeological
spaces

In this final section, we want to sketch one of the many possibilities
that we can start to explore using the Grothendieck topos
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{T}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\text{T}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}
.
The idea is to show that the site
(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty},\Gamma)
is a concrete
site. In this way, considering the space of concrete sheaves over
this site, we get a category of spaces that extends usual smooth manifolds
but it is closed with respect to operations such as: arbitrary subspaces,
products, sums, function spaces, etc. (see
[
57
,
61
,
7
,
33
,
36
]

and references therein for similar approaches). As above, all the
necessary categorical notions will be introduced).

Definition 99
.

Let
\mathbb{D}
be a category and
F:\mathbb{D}^{\text{op}}\longrightarrow\Set

be a functor. Then, we say that
F

is representable
if
F\simeq\mathbb{D}(-,D)

for some
D\in\mathbb{D}
. Moreover, if
(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
is
a site, then we say that
(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)

is a subcanonical
site
if every representable functor
F
is a sheaf
F\in\text{Sh}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
.

Since in Thm.
98
we proved that
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(-,\mathcal{Y})

is a sheaf, directly from Def.
99
it follows that

\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty},\Gamma\right)
is a subcanonical site.

A concrete site is a site whose objects can be thought of
as an underlying set with a structure. The idea is that if
\mathbb{1}\in\mathbb{D}

is a terminal object, then
|D|:=\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{1},D)\in\Set

is the underlying set of
D\in\mathbb{D}
and if
f:C\longrightarrow D
in
\mathbb{D}
,
then
|f|:=\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{1},f):x\in|C|\mapsto x\cdot f=f\circ x\in|D|

is the set-theoretical map corresponding to the arrow
f
. These
maps have a natural relation with covering families of
\Gamma
,
as stated in the following

Definition 100
.

We say that
(\mathbb{D},\Gamma,\mathbb{1})

is a concrete site
if:

(i)

(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
is a subcanonical site.

(ii)

\mathbb{1}\in\mathbb{D}
is a terminal object, i.e.
\mathbb{1}\in\mathbb{D}

and
\forall D\in\mathbb{D}\,\exists!t\in\mathbb{D}(D,\mathbb{1})
.

\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{1},D)=:|D|
is called the
underlying set
of
D\in\mathbb{D}
. For
f\in\mathbb{D}(C,D)
, the map
|f|:=\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{1},f):|C|\longrightarrow|D|

is called the
function associated to the morphism
f
.

(iii)

The functor
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{1},-):\mathbb{D}\longrightarrow\Set

is faithful, i.e. for all
f
,
g\in\mathbb{D}(C,D)
, if
|f|=|g|
,
then
f=g
.

(iv)

If
\left(D_{j}\xrightarrow{\ \ i_{j}\ \ }D\right)_{j\in J}\in\Gamma(D)
,
then the associated maps trivially cover
|D|
, i.e.:

\bigcup_{j\in J}|i_{j}|\left(|D_{i}|\right)=|D|.

(9.6)

For example, let us define a terminal object in the category

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}
of glueable spaces as:

\mathbb{1}:=\left(\{0\}\right)_{\bar{\mathbb{1}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}

where
\bar{\mathbb{1}}=\{*\}
. Note that, if we view
\mathbb{R}^{n}=\Set\left(\left\{1,\ldots,n\right\},\mathbb{R}\right)
,
then
\text{Card}\left(\mathbb{R}^{0}\right)=1
and hence
\text{Card}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{0}\right)=\text{Card}\left(\left(\mathbb{R}^{0}\right)^{I}/\sim_{\rho}\right)=1
.
Therefore,
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{0}=\{0\}
is the trivial ring. It is also a strongly
internal set because
B_{1}(0)=\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{0}\mid|x-0|<1\right\}=\{0\}
.

What is
\varphi\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{1},\mathcal{X})=|\mathcal{X}|

in this case? Set
\varphi=\left(\left(f\right)_{\bar{\mathbb{1}}},\alpha\right)

and
\mathcal{X}=\left(U_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
, then
\alpha:\bar{\mathbb{1}}\longrightarrow J

and
f:\{0\}\longrightarrow U_{\alpha(*)}
, which can be identified with the
pair
(f(0),\alpha(*))\in U_{\alpha(*)}\times\{\alpha(*)\}
. Therefore,

|\mathcal{X}|=\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{1},\mathcal{X})\simeq\sum_{j\in J}U_{j}=\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}\times\{j\}.

Similarly,
\psi=\left(\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J},\alpha\right)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}\left(\left(U_{j}\right)_{j\in J},\left(V_{l}\right)_{l\in L}\right)

can be identified with the map:

|\psi|:(x,j)\in\sum_{j\in J}U_{j}\mapsto\left(f_{j}(x),\alpha(j)\right)\in\sum_{l\in L}V_{l},

i.e. with the map
(x,j)\mapsto\left(f(x),\alpha(j)\right)
, where

f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j},\bigcup_{l\in L}V_{l}\right)

is obtained by gluing
\left(f_{j}\right)_{j\in J}
. This implies
condition Def.
100
.
(iii)
,
whereas Def.
100
.
(iv)
follows
from Rem.
95
.
(ii)
:

Theorem 101
.

\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty},\Gamma\right)
is a concrete
site.

It is well known that a sheaf
F\in\text{Sh}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)

can be thought of as a generalized space defined by the information

F(D)\in\Set
associated to each test space
D\in\mathbb{D}
. The
idea of
concrete sheaf
is that it is this kind of generalized
space defined by an underlying set of points
F(\mathbb{1})
. For
example, any
y\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{1},\mathcal{Y})
can be identified
with the map
\sum_{0}\{0\}\times\{0\}=\{(0,0)\}\longrightarrow\sum_{l\in L}V_{l}
,
and hence
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{1},\mathcal{Y})\simeq\sum_{l\in L}V_{l}
.

Definition 102
.

Let
(\mathbb{D},\Gamma,\mathbb{1})
be a concrete
site. Then we say that
F

is a concrete sheaf
(and we write

F\in\text{CSh}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma,\mathbb{1})
) if:

(i)

F\in\text{Sh}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
.

(ii)

For all
s\in F(D)
, let
\text{$\underline{s}$}:p\in|D|\mapsto F(p)(s)\in F(\mathbb{1})
,
then we have

\forall D\in\mathbb{D}\,\forall s,t\in F(D):\ \underline{s}=\underline{t}\ \Rightarrow\ s=t.

Similarly to what we did above, we can prove that
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{G}\ell^{\infty}(-,\mathcal{Y})

is a concrete sheaf.

10.
Conclusions and future perspectives

Sobolev and Schwartz solved the problem “how to derive continuous
functions?”. Also Sebastiao e Silva (see
[
99
]
) solved the
same problem without relying on functional analysis at all, but instead
using only a formal approach and arriving at an isomorphic solution.
We solved the problem: “how to derive continuous functions obtaining
set-theoretical functions, unrestrictedly composable, extending the
usual classical theorems of calculus and allowing for inifinitesimal
and infinite values?”. This second problem doesn’t appear to have a trivial formal solution.

We have shown that GSF theory has features that closely resemble classical
smooth functions. In contrast, some differences have to be carefully
considered, such as the fact that the new ring of scalars
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

is not a field, it is not totally ordered, it is not order complete,
so that its theory of supremum and infimum is more involved (see
[
82
]
),
and its intervals are not connected in the sharp topology because
the set of all the infinitesimals is a clopen set. Almost all these
properties are necessarily shared by other non-Archimedean rings because
their opposites are incompatible with the existence of infinitesimal
numbers.

Conversely, the ring of Robinson-Colombeau generalized numbers
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

is a framework where the use of infinitesimal and infinite quantities
is available, it is defined using elementary mathematics, and with
a strong connection with infinitesimal and infinite functions of classical
analysis. As proved in
[
28
]
, this leads to a better understanding
and opens the possibility to define new models of physical systems.
We can hence state that GSF theory is potentially a good framework
for mathematical physics.

As we started to see in Sec.
9.5
, the category
of concrete sheaves over the concrete site of gluable families contains
the category of strongly open sets and GSF and hence, also the category
of ordinary smooth functions on open sets. In future works, we will
build on this and show that it also contains the category of smooth
manifolds (more generally all diffeological spaces). This opens the
possibility to study singular differential geometry using non-Archimedean
methods and, as is typical of topos theory, interesting connections
with logic.

Finally, as we will see in the next two papers of this series (
[
76
,
43
]
),
GSF theory is also an interesting non-Archimedian framework for the
mathematical analysis of singular non-linear ordinary and partial
differential equations.
